Our multi-fluid 22-moment model requires only eleven ratios of the Chapman-Cowling integrals
\Omega_{l,j}
(see e.g. (
51
))
where the numbers “l” and “j” never reach or exceed the value of 10, and in the rest
of the paper we use an abbreviated notation
\Omega_{lj}
.

III.6
Collisional frequencies
\nu_{ab}

It is useful to clarify the various collisional frequencies (defined also in (
42
))

\nu_{ab}=\frac{16}{3}\frac{\mu_{ab}}{m_{a}}n_{b}\Omega^{(1,1)}_{ab};\qquad\beta^{2}_{ab}=v_{\textrm{th}a}^{2}+v_{\textrm{th}b}^{2}=\frac{2T_{a}}{m_{a}}+\frac{2T_{b}}{m_{b}}=\frac{2T_{ab}}{\mu_{ab}};\qquad T_{ab}=\frac{T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a}}{m_{a}+m_{b}},

the
T_{ab}
being the reduced temperature. The particular collisional frequencies read

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}}{m_{a}}\mu_{ab}r_{ab}^{2}\beta_{ab};\qquad r_{ab}=r_{a}+r_{b};

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}}{m_{a}\mu_{ab}}(q_{a}q_{b})^{2}\beta_{ab}^{-3}A_{1}(2);\quad\textrm{for}\,\ln\Lambda\gg 1:\,A_{1}(2)=2\ln\Lambda;

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}}{m_{a}}\mu_{ab}^{\frac{\nu-3}{\nu-1}}|K_{ab}|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\beta_{ab}^{\frac{\nu-5}{\nu-1}}\,A_{1}(\nu)\Gamma\big(3-\frac{2}{\nu-1}\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Maxwell molecules:}\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}}{m_{a}}\mu_{ab}^{1/2}|K_{ab}|^{1/2}A_{1}(5)\Gamma\big(\frac{5}{2}\big).

(56)

Note that
\Gamma(5/2)=(3/4)\sqrt{\pi}
and

\mu_{ab}^{\frac{\nu-3}{\nu-1}}\beta_{ab}^{\frac{\nu-5}{\nu-1}}=\mu_{ab}^{1/2}(2T_{ab})^{\frac{(\nu-5)}{2(\nu-1)}}.

The
\nu_{ab}
of Maxwell molecules, where the ion polarizes the neutral (i.e. ion-neutral collisions), is given later by (
411
).
Also note that in general
\nu_{ab}\neq\nu_{ba}
(but one can still of course use the expression (
56
) and simply exchange
the indices to obtain the
\nu_{ba}
expression) and instead the collisional frequencies are related by
the conservation of momentum

m_{a}n_{a}\nu_{ab}=m_{b}n_{b}\nu_{ba},

(57)

which is satisfied because
\Omega_{ab}^{(1,1)}=\Omega_{ba}^{(1,1)}
is true. The expressions simplify for small temperature differences
(where the reduced temperature
T_{ab}\simeq T_{a})

\displaystyle T_{a}\simeq T_{b}\qquad

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}n_{b}\frac{\mu_{ab}^{1/2}}{m_{a}}r_{ab}^{2}(2T_{a})^{1/2};

(58)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}n_{b}\frac{\mu_{ab}^{1/2}}{m_{a}}(q_{a}q_{b})^{2}(2T_{a})^{-3/2}A_{1}(2);

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}n_{b}\frac{\mu_{ab}^{1/2}}{m_{a}}|K_{ab}|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}(2T_{a})^{\frac{\nu-5}{2(\nu-1)}}\,A_{1}(\nu)\Gamma\big(3-\frac{2}{\nu-1}\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Maxwell molecules:}\qquad\nu_{ab}=\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}n_{b}\frac{\mu_{ab}^{1/2}}{m_{a}}|K_{ab}|^{1/2}A_{1}(5)\Gamma\big(\frac{5}{2}\big),

where the masses are represented by a universal factor of
\mu_{ab}^{1/2}/m_{a}
. For self-collisions this factor is just replaced by

\mu_{aa}^{1/2}/m_{a}=1/\sqrt{2m_{a}}
and we only write down the inverse power-law force

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\quad\nu_{aa}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8}{3}\sqrt{\pi}n_{a}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2m_{a}}}|K_{aa}|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}(2T_{a})^{\frac{\nu-5}{2(\nu-1)}}\,A_{1}(\nu)\Gamma\big(3-\frac{2}{\nu-1}\big);

(59)

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{aa}}{\nu_{ab}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big|\frac{K_{aa}}{K_{ab}}\Big|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\frac{n_{a}}{n_{b}}\Big(\frac{m_{a}+m_{b}}{m_{b}}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac{T_{a}}{T_{ab}}\Big)^{\frac{(\nu-5)}{2(\nu-1)}}.

(60)

Note that the hard sphere limit is obtained by
\lim_{\nu\to\infty}|K_{aa}/K_{ab}|^{2/(\nu-1)}=(r_{aa}/r_{ab})^{2}
. Also note that the factor of
\sqrt{2}
is present
in the ratio (
60
). We will later consider further particular cases of
lightweight or heavyweight species “a”, where for the small temperature differences
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}

\displaystyle m_{a}\ll m_{b}:\qquad\frac{\nu_{aa}}{\nu_{ab}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big|\frac{K_{aa}}{K_{ab}}\Big|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\frac{n_{a}}{n_{b}};

\displaystyle m_{a}\gg m_{b}:\qquad\frac{\nu_{aa}}{\nu_{ab}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big|\frac{K_{aa}}{K_{ab}}\Big|^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\frac{n_{a}}{n_{b}}\Big(\frac{m_{a}}{m_{b}}\Big)^{1/2}.

(61)

Using the last result and prescribing Coulomb collisions together with the charge neutrality

n_{e}=Z_{i}n_{i}
then yields the usual ratios

\nu_{ee}/\nu_{ei}=1/(Z_{i}\sqrt{2})
and
\nu_{ii}/\nu_{ie}=Z_{i}(m_{i}/m_{e})^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}
, where the
\sqrt{2}
is naturally present.
For a further discussion about the
\sqrt{2}
factor see Section 8.2, p. 31 in
[
43
]
“Collisional frequencies for ion-electron plasma”.

III.7
Evolution equations for 22-moment model

As in Part 1, the integration of the Boltzmann equation yields the usual “basic” evolution equations

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}n_{a}+n_{a}\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a}=0;

(62)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bm{u}_{a}+\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a}-\bm{G}-\frac{eZ_{a}}{m_{a}}\Big(\bm{E}+\frac{1}{c}\bm{u}_{a}\times\bm{B}\Big)=\frac{\bm{R}_{a}}{\rho_{a}};

(63)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}p_{a}+\frac{5}{3}p_{a}\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a}+\frac{2}{3}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}:(\nabla\bm{u}_{a})=\frac{2}{3}Q_{a},

(64)

which are accompanied by the following evolution equations for the higher-order moments (see Eqs. (9)-(12) of
[
43
]
for the 21-moment model,
Eqs. (130)-(133) for the 22-moment model, or Eqs. (D13)-(D15) for a general “n”)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+\big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\cdot\nabla\bm{u}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}:\nabla\bm{u}_{a})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\bm{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big]+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}}{3}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a};

(65)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{7}{5}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a}+\frac{7}{5}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\cdot\nabla\bm{u}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}(\nabla\bm{u}_{a})\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{6}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a})\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a}-\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a};

(66)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{7}{3}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}(\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a})+4\big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-5\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big):\nabla\bm{u}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{8}{\rho_{a}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a})\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}=\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv Q^{(4)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}Q_{a}-\frac{8}{\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a}\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a};

(67)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a})\Big]+\frac{9}{7}(\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a})\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{9}{7}(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\cdot\nabla\bm{u}_{a})^{S}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{7}\big((\nabla\bm{u}_{a})\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{22}{21}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}:\nabla\bm{u}_{a})-\,\frac{14}{5\rho_{a}}\Big[\big((\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a})\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a})\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+\frac{7}{15}\big(15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\big)\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)*}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}}{3}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)*}_{a}-\frac{14}{5\rho_{a}}\Big[(\bm{R}_{a}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\bm{R}_{a}\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a})\Big];

(68)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\frac{1}{3}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(6)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(6)}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{9}{5}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}(\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a})+\frac{9}{5}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}\cdot\nabla\bm{u}_{a}+\frac{4}{5}(\nabla\bm{u}_{a})\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{7}{3\rho_{a}}\big(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a}\big)\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{4}{\rho_{a}}\big(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{p}}}_{a}\big)\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle\quad=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\bm{R}_{a}-\frac{7}{3\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{4}{\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}.

(69)

As in Part 1, the last equation (
69
) is closed with the following closures for the stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(6)}_{a}
and the scalar

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}_{a}

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(6)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 18\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-63\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a};\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(6)}_{a}=21\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}.

(70)

The reduction into the 21-moment model is obtained easily by neglecting the evolution equation (
67
) and
by simply prescribing
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=0
in the other evolution equations. The left-hand-sides of the evolution equations
contain the direction of the magnetic field
\bm{\hat{b}}=\bm{B}/|\bm{B}|
, the cyclotron frequency of species

\Omega_{a}=q_{a}|\bm{B}|/(m_{a}c)
(which should not be confused with the Chapman-Cowling integrals),
the rate-of-strain tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}=(\nabla\bm{u}_{a})^{S}-(2/3)\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a}
and the symmetric operator “S”, defined as
A_{ij}^{S}=A_{ij}+A_{ji}
.
The above evolution equations were obtained by integrating the Boltzmann equation

\frac{\partial f_{a}}{\partial t}+\bm{v}_{a}\cdot\nabla f_{a}+\Big[\bm{G}+\frac{eZ_{a}}{m_{a}}(\bm{E}+\frac{1}{c}\bm{v}_{a}\times\bm{B})\Big]\cdot\nabla_{v_{a}}f_{a}=C(f_{a}),

(71)

see Appendices A, C, D in Part 1, together with Section 8.7 “Inclusion of Gravity”.

III.8
Coupled evolution equations (semi-linear approximation)

In the semi-linear approximation, where many terms such as
(\nabla p_{a})\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
,
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}(\nabla\cdot\bm{u}_{a})
,

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}
,
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\nabla T_{a}
or
\bm{R}_{a}\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
are neglected, which is equivalent
to prescribing zero large-scale gradients, the evolution equations become (we keep the full convective derivative
d_{a}/dt=\partial/\partial t+\bm{u}_{a}\cdot\nabla
)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\bm{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big]=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(72)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\frac{1}{6}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(73)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}=\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(74)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a})\Big]=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(75)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+18\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-98\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}.

(76)

In these equations, the coupling between the stress-tensors and heat fluxes is retained.
Neglecting the scalars
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
and focusing
only at the 21-moment model, various explicit solutions with the coupled stress-tensors and heat fluxes (for the Coulomb collisions) can be found in Section 6 of Part 1,
where for simplicity only the unmagnetized solutions are given.

III.9
Un-coupled evolution equations

To further simplify the system, one can de-couple the stress-tensors and heat fluxes. The evolution equations for the stress-tensors become

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime};

(77)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime},

(78)

and the evolution equations for the heat fluxes read

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(79)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime},

(80)

representing the 21-moment model, which eventually recovers the Braginskii model (in the quasi-static approximation for a plasma with only one ion species).
As in Part 1, for the 22-moment model one additionally considers the evolution equation

\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}=\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}.

(81)

We note that it is also possible to keep the stress-tensors (
77
)-(
78
) separate as they are now, but
retain the contributions of scalars
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
into the heat flux equations

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+\frac{1}{6}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(82)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime},

(83)

so that one considers system (
81
)-(
83
) instead of (
79
)-(
81
).
As reported by
[
2
,
3
]
for the 1-Hermite unmagnetized case (which in our notation should correspond to
eliminating the eq. (
83
) and prescribing
\vec{\bm{X}}_{a}^{(5)}=28(p_{a}/\rho_{a})\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
in (
81
)),
the quasi-static approximation then yields additional contributions to the heat fluxes, in
the form
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\sim\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\sim\nabla\nabla^{2}T_{a}
, which we neglect for simplicity.

IV
Collisional contributions through Chapman-Cowling integrals

Here we write the collisional contributions by expressing them through the Chapman-Cowling integrals, for arbitrary temperatures and masses.
We keep the notation as close as possible to
[
43
]
, with the difference that here we keep the 1-Hermite and 2-Hermite contribution separate,
so that the 22-moment model can be reduced to simpler models easily (this is further clarified below).

IV.1
Momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{a}

The momentum exchange rates are given by

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{\rho_{a}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{a})+\frac{\mu_{ab}}{T_{ab}}\Big(1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Big)\Big(\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}\Big)

(84)

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\mu_{ab}}{T_{ab}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big)\Big[\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{b}-28\frac{p_{b}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}\Big)\Big]\Big\},

where for later discussion of various collisional processes, it is beneficial to introduce coefficients

V_{ab(0)}=1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12};\qquad V_{ab(3)}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}.

(85)

Alternativelly, re-grouping the usual heat fluxes together

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{\rho_{a}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{a})+\frac{\mu_{ab}}{T_{ab}}\Big[V_{ab(1)}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-V_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}\Big]

(86)

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\mu_{ab}}{T_{ab}}\Big)^{2}V_{ab(3)}\Big[\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{b}\Big]\Big\},

yields coefficients

\displaystyle V_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{28T_{a}m_{b}}{T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a}}\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big);

\displaystyle V_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{28T_{b}m_{a}}{T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a}}\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big).

(87)

For clarity, specifying the particular case of Coulomb collisions (by
\Omega_{12}=1
and
\Omega_{13}=2
), yields

V_{ab(0)}=+3/5
and
V_{ab(3)}=+3/56
, recovering eqs. (15)-(16) of
[
43
]
(Part 1). Previously in Section 2.1 of Part 1,
all of the the collisional contributions were given in the form (
86
), i.e. after
re-grouping the 1-Hermite and 2-Hermite contributions together (and the results before re-grouping can be found in Appendices of Part 1).
Here in Part 2, we prefer to keep the original form (
84
).
The advantage is, that the reduction into the 1-Hermite approximation can be done easily (by prescribing

\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}=28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{b}=28\frac{p_{b}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}
).
All the other collisional contributions will be given only in the form (
84
). We will also adopt the
free wording from Part 1 and call the ratios such as (
87
) simply “mass-ratio coefficients”,
even though they contain masses as well as temperatures and now also the dimensionless
Chapman-Cowling integrals.

IV.2
Energy exchange rates
Q_{a}

The energy exchange rates of the 22-moment model are given by

\displaystyle Q_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{b\neq a}\frac{\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})}\Big\{3(T_{b}-T_{a})+\hat{P}_{ab(1)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{n_{a}p_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\hat{P}_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{b}}{n_{b}p_{b}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{b}\Big\},

(88)

with “mass-ratio coefficients”

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{a}m_{b}(7T_{b}m_{b}+4T_{b}m_{a}-3T_{a}m_{b})}{8(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}-\Omega_{12}\frac{T_{a}m_{b}(7T_{b}m_{b}+2T_{b}m_{a}-5T_{a}m_{b})}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\Omega_{13}\frac{(T_{a}-T_{b})T_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{b}m_{a}(7T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-3T_{b}m_{a})}{8(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}-\Omega_{12}\frac{T_{b}m_{a}(7T_{a}m_{a}+2T_{a}m_{b}-5T_{b}m_{a})}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}

(89)

\displaystyle\quad+\Omega_{13}\frac{(T_{a}-T_{b})T_{b}m_{a}^{2}}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}.

Terms proportional to
|\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{a}|^{2}
are neglected in the semi-linear approximation and for a further discussion, see Section
8.1 “Energy Conservation”, p. 30 in Part 1 (and for the
Q_{ab}
of 5-moment models, see also eq. (
F20
) here).
In the formulation above, each Chapman-Cowling integral has its own mass-ratio coefficient (which for a given
T_{a},T_{b},m_{a},m_{b}
is a pure number).
This is the natural form how to write the results, because the above form comes directly from the calculations (where one naturally splits the results
to create the Chapman-Cowling integrals separately).
Alternatively, the results can be re-arranged so that the Chapman-Cowling integrals are together, yielding

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{a}m_{b}}{2(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big[T_{b}m_{b}\big(-\frac{7}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{13}+\frac{7}{4}\big)+T_{a}m_{b}\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{3}{4}\big)-T_{b}m_{a}\frac{2}{5}(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2})\Big];

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{b}m_{a}}{2(T_{b}m_{a}+T_{a}m_{b})^{2}}\Big[T_{a}m_{a}\big(-\frac{7}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{13}+\frac{7}{4}\big)+T_{b}m_{a}\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{3}{4}\big)-T_{a}m_{b}\frac{2}{5}(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2})\Big].

(90)

As a quick double check, prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers the
\hat{P}_{ab(1)},\hat{P}_{ab(2)}
coefficients eq. (141) in Part 1.

IV.3
Stress tensor exchange rates
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

The collisional exchange rates for the usual stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
are given by

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big)

(91)

\displaystyle+\sum_{b\neq a}\frac{\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}\Big[-K_{ab(1)}\frac{1}{n_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+K_{ab(2)}\frac{1}{n_{b}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{b}

\displaystyle+L_{ab(1)}\frac{1}{n_{a}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big)-L_{ab(2)}\frac{1}{n_{b}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{b}}{p_{b}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{b}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{b}\Big)\Big],

with the 1-Hermite mass-ratio coefficients

\displaystyle K_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{b}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}-\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{b}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}\Omega_{12}+\frac{3m_{b}}{5m_{a}}\Omega_{22};

(92)

\displaystyle K_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}+\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}\Omega_{12}-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}.

(93)

It can be shown that this 1-Hermite viscosity model given by
K_{ab(1)}
;
K_{ab(2)}

is equivalent to eq. (44) of
[
113
]
, or eq. (4.132a) of
[
111
]
, obtained also by
[
14
]
.

The 2-Hermite coefficients read

\displaystyle L_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{a(11)}+L_{a(12)}+L_{a(22)}+L_{a(13)}+L_{a(23)};

(94)

\displaystyle L_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{a}T_{b}m_{b}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};\qquad L_{a(12)}=\Omega_{12}\frac{2T_{a}m_{b}(T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a}-2T_{b}m_{b})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle L_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{22}\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})m_{a}};\qquad L_{a(13)}=\Omega_{13}\frac{4T_{a}(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{b}^{2}}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle L_{a(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{23}\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})m_{a}};

\displaystyle L_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{b(11)}+L_{b(12)}+L_{b(22)}+L_{b(13)}+L_{b(23)};

(95)

\displaystyle L_{b(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};\qquad L_{b(12)}=-\Omega_{12}\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}(2T_{a}m_{a}+T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle L_{b(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{22}\frac{3T_{b}m_{a}}{10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};\qquad L_{b(13)}=-\Omega_{13}\frac{4T_{b}(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}^{2}}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle L_{b(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{23}\frac{3T_{b}m_{a}}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}.

Alternatively, the (
94
)-(
95
) can be re-arranged into

\displaystyle L_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{2T_{a}m_{b}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}m_{a}}\Big\{T_{b}m_{a}^{2}(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2})+T_{a}m_{b}^{2}\big(-\frac{3}{4}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23}\big)

(96)

\displaystyle+m_{a}m_{b}\Big[T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{3}{4}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23}\Big)+(T_{b}-T_{a})\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{7}\Omega_{13}\big)\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle L_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big\{m_{a}\Big[-T_{a}\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2}\big)+T_{b}\big(-\frac{3}{4}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23}\big)+(T_{b}-T_{a})\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{7}\Omega_{13}\big)\Big]

(97)

\displaystyle-m_{b}T_{a}\big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2}+\frac{3}{4}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big\}.

As can be seen, in the re-arranged form (
96
)-(
97
) the
L_{ab(1)}
and
L_{ab(2)}
coefficients are symmetric only partially,
and one keeps re-arranging them back and forth to show this partial symmetry. All higher-order 2-Hermite coefficients
for artibtrary temperatures will be given only in the form (
94
)-(
95
) and a potential
user can rearrange these if needed. For small temperature
differences (see later Section
IV.8
), all of the coefficients will be given in the re-arranged form (
96
)-(
97
).

Note that in the final collisional contributions of Part 1 given there by eqs. (22)-(23), a further re-arrangement is done
for the
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}
by introducing (hat)
\hat{K}_{ab(1)}=K_{ab(1)}+7L_{ab(1)}
and
\hat{K}_{ab(2)}=K_{ab(2)}+7L_{ab(2)}
, and the (non-hat)
coefficients
K_{ab(1)}
&
K_{ab(2)}
are given there by (L27). Now it it is easy to verify that prescribing Coulomb collisions indeed recovers
the equations of Part 1.

IV.4
Higher-order stress tensor exchange rates
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

The collisional exchange rates for the higher-order stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(4)}
are given by

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\big(\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}-\nu_{aa}\big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big)

(98)

\displaystyle+\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big[-{M}_{ab(1)}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+{M}_{ab(2)}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}p_{b}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{b}

\displaystyle\qquad-N_{ab(1)}\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big)-N_{ab(2)}\frac{p_{a}^{2}\rho_{b}}{p_{b}^{2}\rho_{a}}\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{b}-7\frac{p_{b}}{\rho_{b}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{b}\Big)\Big],

with mass-ratio coefficients

\displaystyle{M}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{a(11)}+M_{a(12)}+M_{a(22)}+M_{a(13)}+M_{a(23)}+M_{a(33)};

(99)

\displaystyle M_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{14T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}(2T_{a}m_{a}+T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{a(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{12}\frac{28T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}(4T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-7T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{22}\frac{7T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}(11T_{a}m_{a}+3T_{a}m_{b}-8T_{b}m_{a})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle M_{a(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{13}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}m_{b}^{2}(4T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-8T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{a(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{23}\frac{2m_{b}^{2}(11T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+3T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-22T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+14T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle M_{a(33)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{33}\frac{12m_{a}m_{b}^{2}(T_{b}-T_{a})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};

\displaystyle{M}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{b(11)}+M_{b(12)}+M_{b(22)}+M_{b(13)}+M_{b(23)}+M_{b(33)};

(100)

\displaystyle M_{b(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{14T_{b}m_{a}^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a}-2T_{b}m_{b})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{b(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{12}\frac{28T_{b}m_{a}(3T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-7T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+5T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{b(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{22}\frac{7T_{b}m_{a}^{2}(8T_{a}m_{b}-3T_{b}m_{a}-11T_{b}m_{b})}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle M_{b(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{13}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}(4T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-8T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle M_{b(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{23}\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}(11T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+3T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-22T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+14T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle M_{b(33)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{33}\frac{12(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}}{5(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}T_{a}^{2}};

\displaystyle{N}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{a(11)}+N_{a(12)}+N_{a(22)}+N_{a(13)}+N_{a(23)}+N_{a(33)}+N_{a(14)}+N_{a(24)}+N_{a(34)};

(101)

\displaystyle N_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}(14T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+7T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-4T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-11T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle N_{a(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{12}\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}}{5(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}}\Big(28T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+7T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+7T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-26T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}

\displaystyle-82T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+19T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+47T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{22}\frac{T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}(77T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+21T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-22T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-78T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle N_{a(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{13}\frac{2m_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}}\Big(28T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+7T_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{3}-80T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-178T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle-16T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}-23T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{b}^{3}+146T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+219T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-32T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}-82T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}

\displaystyle-117T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{a(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{23}\frac{m_{b}^{2}}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big(77T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+21T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-121T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-296T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle+21T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+100T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+198T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{a(33)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{33}\frac{6m_{a}m_{b}^{2}(7T_{a}m_{b}-2T_{b}m_{a}-9T_{b}m_{b})}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle N_{a(14)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{14}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}m_{b}^{3}(4T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-8T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle N_{a(24)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{24}\frac{2m_{b}^{3}(11T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+3T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-22T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+14T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle N_{a(34)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{34}\frac{12m_{a}m_{b}^{3}(T_{b}-T_{a})}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle{N}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{b(11)}+N_{b(12)}+N_{b(22)}+N_{b(13)}+N_{b(23)}+N_{b(33)}+N_{b(14)}+N_{b(24)}+N_{b(34)};

(102)

\displaystyle N_{b(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}(11T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-7T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-14T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle N_{b(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{12}\frac{2T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}}{5T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}}\Big(40T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+19T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+7T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-14T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-82T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle\quad-12T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+7T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+35T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{b(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{22}\frac{T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}(78T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+22T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-21T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-77T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{10T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle N_{b(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{13}\frac{2T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}}\Big(94T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}+66T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}+23T_{a}^{3}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}+16T_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad-14T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{4}-244T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}-100T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}+25T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+14T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}+201T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}+82T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-35T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{b(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{23}\frac{T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big(177T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+100T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+21T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-21T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad-296T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-79T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+98T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle N_{b(33)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{33}\frac{6T_{b}^{2}m_{b}m_{a}^{2}(9T_{a}m_{a}+2T_{a}m_{b}-7T_{b}m_{a})}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle N_{b(14)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{14}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}(4T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-8T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle N_{b(24)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{24}\frac{2T_{b}^{2}m_{b}m_{a}^{2}(11T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+3T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-22T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+14T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle N_{b(34)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{34}\frac{12(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}}{35T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}.

Obviously, the 2-Hermite viscosity model is much more complicated than the 1-Hermite model given by eqs. (
92
)-(
93
).
Prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers eqs. (24)-(25) of Part 1, with the (non-hat)
M_{ab(1)}
&
M_{ab(2)}
given by (L46) and further
re-arrangement (L51).

IV.5
Heat flux exchange rates
\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

The exchange rates for the usual heat flux
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
are given by (note that the momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{a}
enter and the
expressions are not further re-arranged)

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big)

(103)

\displaystyle+\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{-D_{ab(1)}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}D_{ab(2)}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}-p_{a}U_{ab(1)}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{a})

\displaystyle+E_{ab(1)}\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big)+E_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{b}}{p_{b}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{b}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}\Big)\Big\}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bm{R}_{a},

with the 1-Hermite mass-ratios coefficients

\displaystyle D_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{a(11)}+D_{a(12)}+D_{a(22)}+D_{a(13)}+D_{a(23)};

(104)

\displaystyle D_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{a}T_{b}(15T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-16T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{2(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle D_{a(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12}\frac{m_{b}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}\Big(15T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+5T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-27T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-62T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle+5T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+22T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+42T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle D_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{22}\frac{2m_{a}m_{b}(5T_{a}m_{b}-2T_{b}m_{a}-7T_{b}m_{b})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle D_{a(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{13}\frac{2m_{b}^{2}(3T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle D_{a(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{23}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle D_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{b(11)}+D_{b(12)}+D_{b(22)}+D_{b(13)}+D_{b(23)};

(105)

\displaystyle D_{b(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{T_{a}m_{b}(16T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+6T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-5T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-15T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{2(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle D_{b(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12}\frac{m_{b}(37T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+22T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+5T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-5T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-62T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-17T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+20T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle D_{b(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{22}\frac{2m_{b}^{2}(7T_{a}m_{a}+2T_{a}m_{b}-5T_{b}m_{a})}{5(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle D_{b(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{13}\frac{2m_{b}^{2}(3T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}=D_{a(13)};

\displaystyle D_{b(23)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{23}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}=D_{a(23)};

\displaystyle U_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{5m_{a}(2T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a}-3T_{b}m_{b})}{2(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})};

(106)

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12}\frac{m_{b}(3T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})};

\displaystyle-\Omega_{22}\frac{2(T_{a}-T_{b})m_{a}m_{b}}{T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}.

Alternatively, these 1-Hermite coefficients can be re-arranged into

\displaystyle D_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big\{T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{4}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)

(107)

\displaystyle+T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}\Big[T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{14}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{5}{2}\Big)+2T_{a}\Big(\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+3m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}\Big[\frac{14}{5}T_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{4}{21}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{22}+\frac{2}{21}\Omega_{23}-\frac{20}{21}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{62}{15}T_{a}T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{6}{31}\Omega_{13}-\frac{3}{31}\Omega_{22}+\frac{2}{31}\Omega_{23}-\frac{25}{31}\Big)+T_{a}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{5}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}\Big[-22T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{11}\Omega_{22}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)+27T_{a}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{25}{18}\Big)\Big]-3T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}\Big\};

\displaystyle D_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{b}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}\Big\{T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}+\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{22}-3\Big)

(108)

\displaystyle+\frac{22}{5}T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}\Big[T_{a}\Big(\Omega_{12}+\frac{7}{11}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{11}\Omega_{23}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)-\frac{17}{22}T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{17}\Omega_{13}+\frac{6}{17}\Omega_{22}-\frac{4}{17}\Omega_{23}-\frac{75}{34}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{37}{5}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big[T_{a}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{6}{37}\Omega_{13}-\frac{40}{37}\Big)-\frac{62}{37}T_{b}T_{a}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{6}{31}\Omega_{13}-\frac{25}{31}-\frac{7}{31}\Omega_{22}+\frac{2}{31}\Omega_{23}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{20}{37}T_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{22}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)\Big]-T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)\Big\};

\displaystyle U_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}\Big\{-5T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}

(109)

\displaystyle-6m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big[T_{a}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{3}\Big)-2T_{a}T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{3}\Omega_{22}-\frac{5}{3}\Big)+\frac{4}{3}T_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{22}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-4m_{a}m_{b}^{2}T_{a}\Big[T_{a}\Big(\Omega_{22}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)+T_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\Omega_{22}+\frac{15}{4}\Big)\Big]-2T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}\Omega_{12}\Big\}.

It can be shown that this 1-Hermite heat flux model is equivalent to eqs. (45)-(49) of
[

, or eqs. (4.132b)-(4.133d)
of
[

[

.

The 2-Hermite coefficients read

\displaystyle{E}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{a(11)}+E_{a(12)}+E_{a(22)}+E_{a(13)}+E_{a(23)}+E_{a(14)}+E_{a(24)};

(110)

\displaystyle E_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{T_{b}T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}(15T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-12T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-22T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{16(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle E_{a(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12}\frac{T_{a}m_{b}}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}\Big(15T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}+5T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{4}-54T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}-94T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}

\displaystyle+12T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}+68T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}+76T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}\Big);

\displaystyle E_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{22}\frac{T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}(5T_{a}m_{b}-4T_{b}m_{a}-9T_{b}m_{b})}{20(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle E_{a(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{13}\frac{T_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{140(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}\Big(42T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+14T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-39T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-128T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle+23T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+34T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+90T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{14}\frac{T_{a}m_{b}^{3}(3T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+4T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{70(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle E_{a(24)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{24}\frac{(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};

\displaystyle{E}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{b(11)}+E_{b(12)}+E_{b(22)}+E_{b(13)}+E_{b(23)}+E_{b(14)}+E_{b(24)};

(111)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}(22T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+12T_{a}m_{b}^{2}-5T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-15T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{16(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle-94T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-44T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+20T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle-128T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-11T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}+56T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big);

IV.6
Higher-order heat flux exchange rates
\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

The exchange rates for the higher-order heat flux
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}

are given by (note that the momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{a}
enter and the expressions are not further re-arranged)

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{28}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big)

(112)

\displaystyle+\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{-F_{ab(1)}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\frac{\rho_{a}}{\rho_{b}}F_{ab(2)}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}+\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}U_{ab(2)}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{a})

\displaystyle-G_{ab(1)}\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big)+G_{ab(2)}\frac{p_{a}}{p_{b}}\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{b}-28\frac{p_{b}}{\rho_{b}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{b}\Big)\Big\}-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\bm{R}_{a},

with mass-ratio coefficients

\displaystyle{F}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{a(11)}+F_{a(12)}+F_{a(22)}+F_{a(13)}+F_{a(23)}+F_{a(33)}+F_{a(14)}+F_{a(24)}+F_{a(34)};

(113)

\displaystyle F_{a(11)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{7T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}(25T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-20T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-32T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+8T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{T_{a}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle F_{a(12)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{12}\frac{14T_{b}m_{b}m_{a}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}\Big(50T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}+30T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}+30T_{a}^{3}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}+10T_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{4}

\displaystyle-85T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{4}-259T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}-71T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-17T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}+116T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}+256T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}

\displaystyle+20T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-48T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{4}-88T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}\Big);

\displaystyle F_{a(22)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{22}\frac{56T_{b}m_{b}m_{a}^{2}(10T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}+5T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-8T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-25T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}-7T_{a}T_{b}m_{b}^{2}+6T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+11T_{b}^{2}m_{a}m_{b})}{5T_{a}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};

\displaystyle F_{a(13)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-56T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+35T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}\Big);

\displaystyle G_{b(14)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{14}\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{4}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}T_{a}}\Big(89T_{a}^{4}m_{a}^{4}m_{b}+54T_{a}^{4}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{2}+80T_{a}^{4}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}+38T_{a}^{4}m_{a}m_{b}^{4}

\displaystyle+42T_{a}^{2}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{5}+522T_{a}^{2}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}m_{b}-30T_{a}^{2}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{2}-6T_{a}^{2}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{3}-28T_{a}T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{5}-372T_{a}T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{4}m_{b}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{35m_{a}^{2}T_{b}(4T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a}-5T_{b}m_{b})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}

(117)

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{12}\frac{28m_{a}m_{b}}{T_{a}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}\Big(2T_{a}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}+2T_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{3}-3T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{3}-9T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}

\displaystyle\qquad+3T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}-3T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{b}^{3}+6T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+12T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-6T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-4T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}-8T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Omega_{22}\frac{56(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}^{2}m_{b}(T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a}-2T_{b}m_{b})}{T_{a}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}

\displaystyle+\Omega_{13}\frac{4m_{b}^{2}}{T_{a}^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}}\Big(5T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-10T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+6T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+2T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle-\Omega_{23}\frac{16(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}m_{b}^{2}(2T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-4T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+3T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{T_{a}^{2}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}}

\displaystyle+\Omega_{33}\frac{16(T_{b}-T_{a})^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}}{T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}}.

IV.7
Scalar exchange rates
\widetilde{Q}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

The exchange rates for the fully contracted scalar perturbation
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
are given by (note that the energy exchange rates
Q_{a}
enter
and the expressions are not further re-arranged)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+3\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{S_{ab(0)}\frac{(T_{b}-T_{a})}{T_{a}}-S_{ab(1)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-S_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{b}}{p_{b}^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{b}\Big\}

\displaystyle-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}Q_{a};

\displaystyle+\frac{20m_{a}^{2}T_{b}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{b(14)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Omega_{14}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}(T_{a}^{2}m_{a}^{2}+T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+2T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+2T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{15T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}};

\displaystyle S_{b(24)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\Omega_{24}\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})^{2}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{4}m_{b}}{15T_{a}^{2}(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}.

Prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers mass-ratio coefficients (M21) and (M23) in Part 1, with further re-arrangement (M26),
finally yielding eqs. (142)-(143) there.

This concludes the description of the general multi-fluid 22-moment model, which is valid for arbitrary temperatures and masses.
A particular collisional process is obtained by simply specifying the
ratio of the Chapman-Cowling integrals
\Omega_{l,j}
, with examples given by (
49
). For Coulomb collisions, the results
are for convenience summarized in Section
V.1
and for the hard spheres in Section
V.2
.
The ratio of collisional frequencies
\nu_{aa}/\nu_{ab}
is further discussed in Section
III.6
.
Even though the general model might appear quite complicated, all of the mass-ratio coefficients are just pure numbers.
Additionally, the model drastically simplifies by considering small temperature differences, which is addressed in the next Section.

IV.8
Collisional contributions for small temperature differences

For small temperature differences, the momentum exchange rates (
84
) remain unchanged.
Alternatively, one can approximate
T_{ab}=T_{a}
, and in the second formulation (
86
) the mass-ratio coefficients become

\displaystyle V_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{28m_{b}}{m_{b}+m_{a}}\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big);

\displaystyle V_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{28m_{a}}{m_{b}+m_{a}}\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big).

(122)

For the energy exchange rates
Q_{a}
(
88
), the mass-ratios simplify into

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(5-2\Omega_{12})m_{b}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{(5-2\Omega_{12})m_{a}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})}.

(123)

The exchange rates
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}
(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}}\Big[m_{b}^{4}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{13}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15}\Big)+\frac{7}{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{13}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{14}-\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}+\frac{32}{245}\Omega_{24}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{16}{35}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{33}-\frac{833}{32}\Omega_{12}+\frac{251}{32}\Omega_{13}-\frac{49}{4}\Omega_{22}+\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1225}{128}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{42}{5}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}+\frac{8}{21}\Omega_{22}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)+5m_{a}^{4}\Big];

\displaystyle G_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}}\Big[-\frac{7}{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{3}\Big(-\frac{32}{245}\Omega_{33}+\frac{12}{5}\Omega_{12}-\frac{251}{245}\Omega_{13}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{245}\Omega_{15}+\frac{32}{35}\Omega_{22}-\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}

(131)

\displaystyle+\frac{32}{245}\Omega_{24}-\frac{10}{7}\Big)+\frac{119}{10}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{4}{17}\Omega_{13}+\frac{4}{119}\Omega_{14}+\frac{8}{17}\Omega_{22}-\frac{16}{119}\Omega_{23}-\frac{30}{17}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{2}m_{a}^{3}m_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{13}-\frac{5}{4}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle U_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\Omega_{13}m_{b}^{2}+28\Omega_{12}m_{a}m_{b}+35m_{a}^{2}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}}.

(132)

Finally, the exchange rates
\widetilde{Q}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}
(
118
) become

\displaystyle S_{ab(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4m_{a}(2\Omega_{12}m_{b}+5m_{a})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle S_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4m_{a}}{3(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}\Big[m_{a}^{2}+m_{a}m_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)-m_{b}^{2}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle S_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4m_{a}^{2}}{3(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}\Big[m_{b}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}-1\Big)-m_{a}\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{5}{2}\Big)\Big].

(133)

Obviously, for small temperature differences the formulation of the 22-moment model through
the Chapman-Cowling integrals is not overly-complicated and actually quite user-friendly.

IV.9
Collisional contributions for self-collisions (only double-check)

The self-collisional contributions were already separated at the front of all the collisional contributions,
with the rest expressed as a
\sum_{b\neq a}
, and the following expressions are thus not needed.
Nevertheless, for clarity and for the convenience of the reader, we provide these expressions as well.
For self-collisions, the
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}
exchange rates simplify into

\displaystyle{K}_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22};\qquad{K}_{aa(2)}=2-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22};

\displaystyle{L}_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23};

\displaystyle{L}_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{12}-\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23},

(134)

yielding self-collisional contributions

\displaystyle-K_{aa(1)}+K_{aa(2)}=-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{22};\qquad L_{aa(1)}-L_{aa(2)}=\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{23}.

(135)

For the
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}
exchange rates:

\displaystyle{M}_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{7}{2}+\frac{7}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{21}{20}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23};

\displaystyle{M}_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{7}{2}+\frac{7}{5}\Omega_{12}-\frac{21}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23};

\displaystyle{N}_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{8}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{80}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{140}\Omega_{24}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{33};

\displaystyle{N}_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{8}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{80}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{140}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{33},

(136)

yielding self-collisional contributions

\displaystyle-M_{aa(1)}+M_{aa(2)}=-\big(\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big);\qquad N_{aa(1)}+N_{aa(2)}=\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}.

(137)

For the
\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
exchange rates:

\displaystyle D_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{13};\qquad D_{aa(2)}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{13};

\displaystyle E_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{320}\Omega_{12}+\frac{9}{1120}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{560}\Omega_{14}+\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{140}\Omega_{23}+\frac{7}{128};

\displaystyle E_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{7}{320}\Omega_{12}-\frac{9}{1120}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{560}\Omega_{14}+\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{140}\Omega_{23}-\frac{7}{128},

(138)

yielding self-collisional contributions

\displaystyle-D_{aa(1)}+D_{aa(2)}=-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22};\qquad E_{aa(1)}+E_{aa(2)}=\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}.

(139)

For the
\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
exchange rates:

\displaystyle F_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{49}{20}\Omega_{12}+\frac{9}{10}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{14}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{49}{8};

\displaystyle F_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{49}{20}\Omega_{12}-\frac{9}{10}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{14}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{49}{8};

\displaystyle G_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{112}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{280}\Omega_{15}+\frac{1}{35}\Omega_{24}+\frac{1}{35}\Omega_{33}-\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{93}{128};

\displaystyle G_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{112}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{280}\Omega_{15}-\frac{1}{35}\Omega_{24}+\frac{1}{35}\Omega_{33}+\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{93}{128},

(140)

yielding self-collisional contributions

\displaystyle F_{aa(1)}+F_{aa(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{28}{5}\Omega_{22};\qquad-G_{aa(1)}+G_{aa(2)}=-\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}+\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}.

(141)

Finally, for the
\widetilde{Q}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}
exchange rates:

\displaystyle S_{aa(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{15}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{15}\Omega_{22};\qquad S_{aa(2)}=+\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{15}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{15}\Omega_{22},

(142)

yielding self-collisional contributions

S_{aa(1)}+S_{aa(2)}=\frac{2}{15}\Omega_{22}.

(143)

This concludes the formulation of the multi-fluid 22-moment model through the Chapman-Cowling integrals.
Below we briefly consider the particular cases of Coulomb collisions and hard spheres.

V
Collisional contributions for particular cases

V.1
Coulomb collisions (arbitrary temperatures and masses,
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
)

Because in the present formulation the 1-Hermite and 2-Hermite contributions were kept separately,
we often had to reference to various Appendices of Part 1. Considering the Coulomb collisions, it is beneficial to summarize the
entire multi-fluid 22-moment model at one place, which we do right here. The results assume that the Coulomb logarithm is large,
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
.
If this is not necessarily true,
additional corrections for the
\ln\Lambda
can be obtained from the previous general case by employing
Chapman-Cowling integrals (
49
), which contain the coefficients
A_{1}(2)
,
A_{2}(2)
and
A_{3}(2)
given by (
37
).

The momentum exchange rates (
84
)-(
85
) are given by the coefficients

\displaystyle V_{ab(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{5};\qquad V_{ab(3)}=+\frac{3}{56}.

(144)

The energy exchange rates
Q_{a}
(
88
) are given by

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}(5T_{b}m_{b}+4T_{b}m_{a}-T_{a}m_{b})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{3T_{b}m_{a}(5T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}.

(145)

The stress tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
exchange rates
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}
(
91
) have coefficients

\displaystyle K_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{b}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}-\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{b}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}+\frac{6m_{b}}{5m_{a}};

(146)

\displaystyle K_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}+\frac{4(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}-\frac{6}{5};

\displaystyle L_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}(2T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}+3T_{a}m_{b}^{2}+7T_{b}m_{a}^{2}+8T_{b}m_{a}m_{b})}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}m_{a}};

\displaystyle L_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{a}T_{b}(5T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{35(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}.

\displaystyle+70T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{3}+14T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-35T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}\Big);

\displaystyle M_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{2T_{b}m_{a}(9T_{a}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}-2T_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-21T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}^{2}-25T_{a}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}+7T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2})}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}T_{a}(m_{b}+m_{a})};

V.1.1
Coulomb collisions (small temperature differences)

Considering Coulomb collisions with small temperature differences, the model given in the previous section simplifies into

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{b}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{3m_{a}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2(5m_{a}+3m_{b})}{5m_{a}};\qquad K_{ab(2)}=\frac{4}{5};

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{b}(7m_{a}+3m_{b})}{35m_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})};\qquad L_{ab(2)}=\frac{12m_{a}}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})};

(152)

\displaystyle M_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2(35m_{a}^{2}+35m_{a}m_{b}+12m_{b}^{2})}{5(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};\qquad M_{ab(2)}=\frac{4m_{a}(7m_{a}+m_{b})}{5(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle N_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{140m_{a}^{3}+7m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-25m_{a}m_{b}^{2}-12m_{b}^{3}}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};\qquad N_{ab(2)}=\frac{12m_{a}^{2}(7m_{a}-3m_{b})}{35(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};

(153)

\displaystyle U_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{(5m_{a}+2m_{b})}{2(m_{a}+m_{b})};

\displaystyle D_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{30m_{a}^{2}+m_{a}m_{b}-2m_{b}^{2}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};\qquad D_{ab(2)}=-\,\frac{3m_{b}(5m_{a}-4m_{b})}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle E_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{b}(84m_{a}^{2}+7m_{a}m_{b}-2m_{b}^{2})}{560(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};\qquad E_{ab(2)}=\frac{15m_{a}m_{b}(m_{a}-2m_{b})}{112(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};

(154)

\displaystyle U_{ab(2)}=\frac{35m_{a}^{2}+28m_{a}m_{b}+8m_{b}^{2}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle G_{ab(1)}=\frac{1400m_{a}^{4}-1736m_{a}^{3}m_{b}-675m_{a}^{2}m_{b}^{2}-172m_{a}m_{b}^{3}-8m_{b}^{4}}{280(m_{a}+m_{b})^{4}};

\displaystyle S_{ab(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4m_{a}(5m_{a}+2m_{b})}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{2}};

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2m_{a}(10m_{a}^{2}-7m_{a}m_{b}-2m_{b}^{2})}{15(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}};\qquad S_{ab(2)}=\frac{2m_{a}^{3}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})^{3}}.

Hard spheres collisions (arbitrary temperatures and masses)

)-(

The energy exchange rates

(

The stress tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
exchange rates

(

) have coefficients

\displaystyle K_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{b}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}-\frac{12(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{b}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}+\frac{6m_{b}}{5m_{a}};

(159)

\displaystyle K_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{a}(m_{a}+m_{b})}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}+\frac{12(T_{b}-T_{a})m_{a}}{5(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})}-\frac{6}{5};

\displaystyle L_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

V.2.1
Hard spheres collisions (small temperature differences)

The model significantly simplifies for small temperature differences, and it is given by

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{b}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=-\,\frac{m_{a}}{10(m_{a}+m_{b})};

VI
Self-collisions (only one species)

VI.1
Viscosity-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}
(self-collisions)

Here we consider a “simple gas”, where only the self-collisions “a-a” are retained
and collisions with other species are neglected, analogously to the Braginskii ion species.
From the collisional contributions (
91
) and (
98
), the evolution equations for the stress-tensors read

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big);

(170)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\big(\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}

(171)

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big).

As a quick double check, prescribing Coulomb collisions (with
\Omega_{22}=2
,
\Omega_{23}=4
,
\Omega_{24}=12
) recovers
equations (67)-(68) of Part 1. Neglecting the entire evolution equation (
171
) and also the last term of (
170
)
(with a closure
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(4)}=7(p_{a}/\rho_{a})\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
), yields the 1-Hermite approximation of
[
113
,
112
]
and
[
14
]
,
further discussed below in Section
VI.1.1
. Here in the 2-Hermite approximation, the equations (
170
)-(
171
) can be slightly re-arranged into

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{33}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}\Big)\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a};

(172)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{77}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{24}\Big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\Big)\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a},

which can be compared with eqs. (69) of Part 1.
Then, prescribing the quasi-static/highly-collisional approximation
(by canceling the time-derivative
d_{a}/dt
) and solving the coupled system,
yields the stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}
in the usual Braginskii form

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta_{0}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{0}-\eta_{1}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{1}-\eta_{2}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{2}+\eta_{3}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{3}+\eta_{4}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{4};

(173)

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}\big(\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}:\bm{\hat{b}}\bm{\hat{b}}\big)\Big(\bm{\hat{b}}\bm{\hat{b}}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}}{3}\Big);

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}_{\perp}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}_{\perp}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}:\bm{\hat{b}}\bm{\hat{b}}\big)\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}_{\perp};\qquad\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{2}=\big(\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}_{\perp}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{b}}\bm{\hat{b}}\big)^{S};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\bm{I}}}_{\perp}\big)^{S};\qquad\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{4}=\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}\cdot\bm{\hat{b}}\bm{\hat{b}}\big)^{S},

and now the viscosity coefficients read

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{6}\frac{(301\Omega_{22}-84\Omega_{23}+12\Omega_{24})}{(77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2})}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};

(174)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}x^{2}+\frac{3}{196000}\big(301\Omega_{22}-84\Omega_{23}+12\Omega_{24}\big)\big(77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{3}+x\Big[\frac{2353}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{33}{40}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{81}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{3433}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{201}{200}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{99}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(175)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(\frac{3}{700}\Big)^{2}\Big[77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big]^{2}.

As before, the parameter which Braginskii uses to describe the strength of the magnetic field
(sometimes called the Hall parameter)
x=\Omega_{a}/\nu_{aa}
is present
and the usual relations for viscosities hold as well,
\eta_{1}^{a}(x)=\eta_{2}^{a}(2x)
;
\eta_{3}^{a}(x)=\eta_{4}^{a}(2x)
.

Results (
173
)-(
175
) represent the Braginskii ion stress-tensor expressed through the Chapman-Cowling integrals, which enter through
the ratios
\Omega_{l,j}
and through the collisional frequencies
\nu_{aa}
.
The parallel viscosity
\eta_{0}^{a}
(
174
) can be also used
in the unmagnetized case (with solution
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}=-\eta_{0}^{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}
) and it therefore has a general validity for a large class of self-collisional processes.
In contrast, the magnetized viscosities
\eta_{1}^{a}-\eta_{4}^{a}
are valid only for Coulomb collisions and to obtain a more general result, one should obtain the quasi-static
approximation by considering two species of charged particles and neutral particles, where the results will naturally get more complicated
(see Appendix
F.6
).

The Braginskii ion stress-tensor is recovered by prescribing Coulomb collisions (
\Omega_{22}=2
,
\Omega_{23}=4
and
\Omega_{24}=12
) in (
174
)-(
175
)
and by associating the
\nu_{aa}
with the Coulomb collisional frequency, yielding

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions},(\ln\Lambda\gg 1):\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1025}{1068}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\quad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{79321}{19600}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{267}{175}\Big)^{2};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(\frac{6}{5}x^{2}+\frac{10947}{4900}\Big);

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(x^{3}+\frac{46561}{19600}x\Big),

(176)

recovering the analytic viscosities eq. (73) in Part 1, or the numerical viscosities eq. (4.44) in
[
11
]
.
This is a useful re-derivation of the Braginskii model directly through the Boltzmann operator.

Generalization to moderatelly-coupled plasmas reads

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1025}{534}\frac{A_{1}(2)}{A_{2}(2)}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\quad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{79321}{78400}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{267}{700}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{4};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{3}{5}\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}x^{2}+\frac{10947}{39200}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{3}\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{3}+\frac{46561}{78400}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}x\Big],

(177)

where as a reminder, the corrections of the Coulomb logarithm are given by

A_{1}(2)=\ln(\Lambda^{2}+1);\qquad A_{2}(2)=2\ln(\Lambda^{2}+1)-2+\frac{2}{\Lambda^{2}+1}.

(178)

The Braginskii case for weakly-coupled plasmas (
176
) is recovered by
A_{2}(2)/A_{1}(2)=2
.
Note that the definition of the collisional frequency
\nu_{aa}
contains the
A_{1}(2)
coefficient as well, see eq. (
56
),
so if the definition of
\nu_{aa}
is used in (
177
), the
A_{1}(2)
coefficient cancels out and only the
A_{2}(2)
coefficient remains.

Even better use of (
175
) would be to evaluate it with the Debye screened potential, but we
do not provide the Chapman-Cowling integrals for this case (see Section
X.6.3
, asymptotic limit for large temperatures can be found in
[
66
]
).
Obviously, expressing the entire magnetized Braginskii model through the Chapman-Cowling integrals is useful and physically meaningful,
even if one is interested only in the Coulomb collisions.

Now, we should continue by evaluating only the parallel viscosity for the inverse power-law force
F_{ab}=\pm|K_{ab}|/r^{\nu}
(where the attractive force has a repulsive core).
Nevertheless, it feels slightly boring not to evaluate the magnetized viscosities as well, so we will evaluate them anyway, yielding

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{6}\frac{(205\nu^{2}-458\nu+301)}{(101\nu-113)(3\nu-5)}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};

(179)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{\frac{3}{5}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{3}\frac{3}{196000}\frac{(101\nu-113)(3\nu-5)^{3}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}(205\nu^{2}-458\nu+301)\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{3}+\frac{x}{78400}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big(42529\nu^{4}-193828\nu^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+356358\nu^{2}-305956\nu+103201\Big)\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\frac{x^{2}}{78400}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big(71257\nu^{4}-312772\nu^{3}+548886\nu^{2}-449092\nu+144025\Big)

(180)

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{3}{700}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{4}\,\frac{(101\nu-113)^{2}(3\nu-5)^{4}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}.

Here, the parallel viscosity (
179
) is valid for any power-law index
\nu
.
The magnetized viscosities are unfortunatelly valid only for
\nu=2
(and to get more general results one should consider coupling between charged and neutral particles),
nevertheless, these expressions are at least useful as an a posteriori double-check that the models are formulated correctly.

Finally, the case of hard spheres is obtained by prescribing limit
\nu\to\infty
together with
A_{2}(\infty)/A_{1}(\infty)=2/3
in (
179
),
or equivalently, prescribing
\Omega_{22}=2
;
\Omega_{23}=8
;
\Omega_{24}=40
in (
174
).
Out of curiosity, let us consider the slightly academic case of generalized “hard spheres” discussed in Section
II.3

(that have a non-zero cyclotron frequency and feel the magnetic field) and evaluate the magnetized viscosities as well, yielding

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres'':}\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1025}{1212}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\quad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{71257}{19600}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{303}{175}\Big)^{2};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(\frac{6}{5}x^{2}+\frac{12423}{4900}\Big);

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(x^{3}+\frac{42529}{19600}x\Big).

(181)

Note the perhaps surprising numerical similarities between the Coulomb collisions and hard spheres (
176
)-(
181
).

VI.1.1
Reduction into 1-Hermite approximation

In the 1-Hermite approximation, the stress-tensor evolves according to

\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}=-\frac{3}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}.

(182)

By applying the quasi-static approximation, the stress-tensor has the same form (
173
), but now with the 1-Hermite viscosities

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{3\Omega_{22}}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\big[\eta_{2}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{3\Omega_{22}/5}{x^{2}+(3\Omega_{22}/5)^{2}};\qquad\big[\eta_{4}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{x}{x^{2}+(3\Omega_{22}/5)^{2}}.

(183)

To emphasize that the results (
183
) represent viscosities in the simplified 1-Hermite approximation, we have added
the brackets
[\ldots]_{1}
around the viscosity coefficients. Similarly, the previously given viscosities in the 2-Hermite approximation
(
174
)-(
181
) can be denoted by putting the brackets
[\ldots]_{2}
around them.
We use this notation only in the particular sub-sections, where the comparison to the 1-Hermite approximation is made and otherwise the
2-Hermite designation
[\ldots]_{2}
is ommited.
An analogous notation is used by
[
18
]
to describe their “first approximation” and “second approximation”.

Note that for both the Coulomb collisions and the hard spheres the
\Omega_{22}=2
, so the entire 1-Hermite stress-tensor (
183
)
is identical for both cases (and only the collisional frequencies are different).
Also note that in the limit of strong magnetic field (
x\gg 1
), the 2-Hermite perpendicular viscosities
\eta_{2}^{a}
and
gyroviscosities
\eta_{4}^{a}
(
175
) are identical to the 1-Hermite results (
183
)

\displaystyle\textrm{Strong B-field:}\qquad\big[\eta_{2}^{a}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{2}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{3\Omega_{22}}{5}\frac{p_{a}\nu_{aa}}{\Omega_{a}^{2}};\qquad\big[\eta_{4}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{4}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\Omega_{a}},

(184)

and only the parallel viscosities
\eta_{0}^{a}
remain different.

For the parallel viscosities, the improvement of the 2-Hermite
approximation with respect to the 1-Hermite approximation can be written in the following form (valid for any collisional process)

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{3}{2}\frac{(7\Omega_{22}-2\Omega_{23})^{2}}{(77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2})}\Big),

(185)

and the result does not contain the collisional frequency
\nu_{aa}
.
Evaluating the result (
185
) for our collisional forces then yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{27}{178}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\qquad\,\,\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{3}{202}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\qquad\,\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{3(\nu-5)^{2}}{2(\nu-1)(101\nu-113)}\Big).

(186)

Interestingly, the equation (
186
) for the inverse power-law force can be found in
[
18
]
, p. 173,
implying that for the self-collisions, our 2-Hermite approximation is identical to the “second approximation” of the last reference.

An interested reader may plot the correction ratio given by (
186
) with respect to
\nu
. Considering only
\nu\geq 2
,
the correction ratio is always non-negative and the largest correction (of
\sim 15\%
) is
indeed obtained for the case of the Coulomb collisions (i.e. the Braginskii case).
The correction ratio then sharply decreases (already for
\nu=3
the correction is only 3/190) and
