becomes identicaly zero for the case of the Maxwell molecules (
\nu=5
) and then again slowly increases until the case of
the hard spheres (
\nu=\infty
) is reached, with a small correction of only
\sim 1.5\%
.
Interestingly, the result (
186
) also implies that Chapman-Cowling essentially did know the 2-Hermite parallel ion viscosity much before Braginskii,
and in a fully analytic form.
The same conclusion will be reached for the parallel 2-Hermite thermal conductivity of the ion species, given later by (
207
).

VI.2
Higher-order viscosity-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}
(self-collisions)

The magnetized solution has a general form

\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[-\eta_{0}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{0}-\eta_{1}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{1}-\eta_{2}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{2}+\eta_{3}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{3}+\eta_{4}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{4}\Big],

(187)

and for the unmagnetized case
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}=-\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\eta_{0}^{a(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}
.
The viscosities (of the 4th-order fluid moment) read

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\frac{35}{6}\frac{(385\Omega_{22}-108\Omega_{23}+12\Omega_{24})}{(77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2})};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{\frac{3}{5}\Big(\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{22}+\Omega_{23}\Big)x^{2}+\frac{3}{28000}\big(385\Omega_{22}-108\Omega_{23}+12\Omega_{24}\big)\big(77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{7x^{3}+x\Big[\frac{22099}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{741}{100)}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{147}{200}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{99}{100}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{700}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{3433}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{201}{200}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{99}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(188)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(\frac{3}{700}\Big)^{2}\Big[77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big]^{2},

where the denominator
\Delta
is the same as for the
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}
in (
175
). Evaluation for the Coulomb collisions yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\eta_{0}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8435}{1068}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+(79321/19600)x^{2}+(267/175)^{2};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big((33/5)x^{2}+(64347/3500)\Big);

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(7x^{3}+(59989/2800)x\Big),

(189)

recovering eq. (76) of Part 1. Evaluation for the generalized “hard spheres” yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres'':}\qquad\eta_{0}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{6755}{1212}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+(71257/19600)x^{2}+(303/175)^{2};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(9x^{2}+(58479/3500)\Big);

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big(7x^{3}+(38053/2800)x\Big).

(190)

Note the numerical similarities between the (
189
) and (
190
). Finally, for the inverse power-law force

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse force:}\qquad\eta_{0}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{35}{6}\frac{(193\nu^{2}-386\nu+241)}{(101\nu-113)(3\nu-5)}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{3}{10}\frac{(3\nu-5)(15\nu-19)}{(\nu-1)^{2}}x^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{3}\frac{3}{28000}\frac{(101\nu-113)(3\nu-5)^{3}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}(193\nu^{2}-386\nu+241)\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big\{7x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}}{11200(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big(38053\nu^{4}-157444\nu^{3}

(191)

\displaystyle\qquad+271182\nu^{2}-224548\nu+75061\Big)\Big\},

where the
\Delta
is equal to (
180
). As a quick double-check, in the limit of weak magnetic field (
x\ll 1
) the
perpendicular viscosity
\eta_{2}^{a(4)}
converges to the parallel viscosity
\eta_{0}^{a(4)}
, as it should.

VI.3
Heat flux
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
(self-collisions)

For the heat flux vectors, the collisional contributions are given by (
103
) and (
112
) and considering only self-collisions,
the evolution equations read

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big);

(192)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{28}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

(193)

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big).

Neglecting the entire (
193
) and also the last term of (
192
), with a closure
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}=28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
,
yields the 1-Hermite approximation of
[
113
,
112
]
and
[
14
]
, discussed further below in Section
VI.3.1
. Here with the
2-Hermite approximation, the equations can be slightly re-arranged into

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{9}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Big)\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a};

(194)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+14\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{24}\Big)\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

(195)

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}.

As a quick double-check, prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers eqs. (39)-(41) of Part 1.
Prescribing the quasi-static approximation (cancelling the
d_{a}/dt
) then yields the heat flux

\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}=-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a},

(196)

with the thermal conductivities

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25(77\Omega_{22}-28\Omega_{23}+4\Omega_{24})}{16(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2})}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

(197)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\Omega_{22}x^{2}+\frac{1}{1225}\big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\big(77\Omega_{22}-28\Omega_{23}+4\Omega_{24}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{149}{40}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{13}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{11}{35}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{2}{245}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{193}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{22}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{4}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(198)

\displaystyle+\Big[\frac{4}{175}\big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\Big]^{2},

where again
x=\Omega_{a}/\nu_{aa}
. The result (
197
) represents parallel thermal conductivity for a general collisional process.
Prescribing Coulomb collisions (
\Omega_{22}=2
,
\Omega_{23}=4
,
\Omega_{24}=12
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions},(\ln\Lambda\gg 1):\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{125}{32}}_{3.906}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[2x^{2}+\frac{648}{245}\Big];

(199)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{3}+\frac{2277}{490}x\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{3313}{1225}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{144}{175}\Big)^{2},

recovering eq. (43) of Part 1 and eq. (4.40) of
[
11
]
.

Generalization to moderatelly-coupled plasmas reads

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{125}{16}\frac{A_{1}(2)}{A_{2}(2)}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}x^{2}+\frac{81}{245}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{3}\Big];

(200)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{3}+\frac{2277}{1960}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}x\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\frac{3313}{4900}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{36}{175}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{4},

where the corrections of the Coulomb logarithm are given by (
178
), and the limit of weakly-coupled plasmas
A_{2}(2)/A_{1}(2)=2
recovers (
199
).

Prescribing the generalized hard spheres
(the parallel conductivity is fully meaningful) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1125}{352}}_{3.196}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[2x^{2}+\frac{792}{245}\Big];

(201)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{3}+\frac{2053}{490}x\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{573}{245}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{176}{175}\Big)^{2}.

Finally, prescribing the inverse power-law force yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25(45\nu^{2}-106\nu+77)}{16(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{3}\frac{(45\nu^{2}-106\nu+77)(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)^{3}}{1225(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(2053\nu^{4}-9876\nu^{3}+19454\nu^{2}-18004\nu+6629)}{1960(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(2865\nu^{4}-13348\nu^{3}+25446\nu^{2}-22820\nu+8113)}{4900(\nu-1)^{6}}

(202)

\displaystyle+\Big[\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\,\frac{4(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)^{2}}{175(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big]^{2},

and prescribing
\nu=2
and
\nu\to\infty
of course recovers results (
199
)-(
201
).

VI.3.1
Reduction into 1-Hermite approximation

In the 1-Hermite approximation, the heat flux evolution equation reads

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}.

(203)

Cancelling the
d_{a}/dt
then yields
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
in the same form (
196
), but now with the 1-Hermite (self-collisional) thermal conductivities

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25}{4\Omega_{22}}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\big[\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}\,\frac{\Omega_{22}}{x^{2}+\big(2\Omega_{22}/5\big)^{2}};\qquad\big[\kappa_{\times}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}\,\frac{(5/2)x}{x^{2}+\big(2\Omega_{22}/5\big)^{2}}.

(204)

Note that only the
\Omega_{22}
is again present and so for the Coulomb collisions and the generalized hard spheres the entire 1-Hermite heat flux has the same form
(and only the
\nu_{aa}
are different). In the limit of strong magnetic field (
x\gg 1
), the 2-Hermite perpendicular and cross-conductivities (
198
)
become identical to the 1-Hermite ones

\displaystyle\textrm{Strong B-field:}\qquad\big[\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\big]_{1}=\Omega_{22}\frac{p_{a}\nu_{aa}}{m_{a}\Omega_{a}^{2}};\qquad\big[\kappa_{\times}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\kappa_{\times}^{a}\big]_{1}=\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\Omega_{a}},

(205)

and only the parallel conductivities
\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}
remain different.

For the parallel conductivities, the difference between the 2-Hermite and 1-Hermite approximation can be written as

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{(7\Omega_{2,2}-2\Omega_{2,3})^{2}}{4(7\Omega_{2,2}^{2}+\Omega_{2,2}\Omega_{2,4}-\Omega_{2,3}^{2})}\Big),

(206)

which again does not contain the collisional frequency
\nu_{aa}
. Evaluating (
206
) for our collisional forces yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{1}{4}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\qquad\,\,\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{1}{44}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\qquad\,\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{2}=\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}}{4(\nu-1)(11\nu-13)}\Big).

(207)

Equation (
207
) can be found in
[
18
]
, p. 173, and plotting the correction ratio with respect to
\nu
yields
similar behavior than the viscosity (
186
). For
\nu\geq 2
, the correction ratio is the largest for the case of the
Coulomb collisions (with a correction of
\sim 25\%
), then the correction sharply decreases (it is only 1/40 for the case
\nu=3
), becomes
identically zero for
\nu=5
, and then slowly increases until the case of hard spheres is reached, with a small correction of only
\sim 2.3\%
.
Again, it is obvious that Chapman-Cowling knew the 2-Hermite parallel ion viscosity value much before Braginskii.

Also note that for the 1-Hermite approximation, the ratio of the parallel thermal conductivity and viscosity

\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big]_{1}/\big[\eta_{0}^{a}\big]_{1}=15/(4m_{a})
, meaning that the ratio is the same regardless of the considered collisional process.

VI.4
Higher-order heat flux
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
(self-collisions)

The solution for the heat flux
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
has a form

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{X}}_{a}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a}\Big],

(208)

with the thermal conductivities (of the 5th-order fluid moment)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{175(91\Omega_{22}-32\Omega_{23}+4\Omega_{24})}{4(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2})}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\big(14\Omega_{22}+4\Omega_{23}\big)x^{2}+\frac{4}{175}\big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\big(91\Omega_{22}-32\Omega_{23}+4\Omega_{24}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[70x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{1253}{10}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{422}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{48}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{72}{5}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{24}{7}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{8}{35}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{193}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{22}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{4}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(209)

\displaystyle+\Big[\frac{4}{175}\big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\Big]^{2},

where the
\Delta
is equal to (
198
). Prescribing the Coulomb collisions yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{2975}{24}}_{123.96}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[44x^{2}+(14688/175)\Big];

(210)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[70x^{3}+x(1086/7)\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+x^{2}(3313/1225)+(144/175)^{2},

recovering eq. (46) of
[
43
]
. Prescribing the generalized hard spheres yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{7525}{88}}_{85.511}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[60x^{2}+(15136/175)\Big];

(211)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[70x^{3}+x(3814/35)\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+x^{2}(573/245)+(176/175)^{2}.

Prescribing the inverse power-law force yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{175(43\nu^{2}-94\nu+67)}{4(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{2(3\nu-5)(15\nu-19)}{(\nu-1)^{2}}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{3}\frac{4(43\nu^{2}-94\nu+67)(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)^{3}}{175(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\,\Big[70x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(1907\nu^{4}-8676\nu^{3}+16570\nu^{2}-15124\nu+5579)}{70(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big],

(212)

with the
\Delta
equal to (
202
).

VII
Case
m_{a}\ll m_{b}
(lightweight particles such as electrons)

Here we will assume
m_{a}\ll m_{b}
and
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}
, which for the Coulomb collisions corresponds to the electron species of Braginskii.
To make this case easily distinguishable from the previous results, we will use a species index “a=e”,
even though the results have a general validity for any particles “e” that have a small mass with respect to particles “b”, and not just the electrons.
For example, one can consider very light hard spheres, which collide with much heavier hard spheres and
as a reminder, we will sometimes write “electrons”.

This case is more complicated than the self-collisional case, because in addition to the “e-e” collisions, one also needs to take into account the
“e-b” collisions. As a consequence, in addition to the collisional frequency
\nu_{ee}
, the expressions will also contain
the collisional frequency
\nu_{eb}
. For general masses, the ratio of
\nu_{aa}/\nu_{ab}
is given by (
60
),
which here simplifies into

\displaystyle\textrm{General}\,(m_{e}\ll m_{b}):\qquad\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{n_{e}}{n_{b}}\frac{\Omega_{ee}^{(1,1)}}{\Omega_{eb}^{(1,1)}};

(213)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{n_{e}}{Z_{b}^{2}n_{b}}=\frac{1}{Z_{b}\sqrt{2}};\qquad(Z_{b}\,\,\textrm{is ion charge});

(214)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\quad\,\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{4r_{e}^{2}}{(r_{e}+r_{b})^{2}}\frac{n_{e}}{n_{b}};\qquad(r_{e},r_{b}\,\,\textrm{are spheres radii})

(215)

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power:}\qquad\quad\,\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Big(\frac{K_{ee}}{K_{eb}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\frac{n_{e}}{n_{b}},

(216)

where for the Coulomb collisions the charge-neutrality
n_{e}=Z_{b}n_{b}
was assumed.
Note that no assumption was made about the hard sphere radius
r_{e}
(i.e. it is not necesserily small with respect to
r_{b}
) and one can for example
consider the particular case of
r_{e}=r_{b}
and
n_{e}=n_{b}
, with the ratio
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/\sqrt{2}
, where the same ratio is obtained for the Coulomb collisions with
the ion charge
Z_{b}=1
. Another simple example is the limit of the Lorentzian gas, where the “e-e” collisions become
insignificant and the ratio
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\ll 1
. For the Coulomb collisions, this corresponds to a large ion charge
Z_{b}\gg 1

and for the hard spheres, this corresponds to for example
r_{e}\ll r_{b}
and
n_{e}\simeq n_{b}
, or
r_{e}\simeq r_{b}
and
n_{e}\ll n_{b}
.
The Lorentzian limit is a bit academical (for example ions with
Z_{b}\gg 1
are encountered much less frequently than the usual ions),
nevertheless, the limit is meaningful. It is just preferable to write it as
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\ll 1
and not as
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\to 0
,
because as the
\nu_{eb}
increases, the final viscosities
\eta_{0}^{e}
and thermal conductivities
\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}
decrease towards zero.
We will of course derive the “electron” stress-tensors and heat fluxes for a general ratio
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}
.

VII.1
Stress-tensors
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}
and
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}

Starting with the stress-tensors, the collisional contributions
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}
and
\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}
given by (
91
) and (
98
)
contain the mass-ratio coefficients for small temperature differences (
124
)-(
127
), which for
m_{e}\ll m_{b}
simplify into

\displaystyle{K}_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\frac{m_{b}}{m_{e}}\Omega_{22};\qquad{K}_{eb(2)}=2-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22};

\displaystyle L_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{b}}{m_{e}}\big(\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{23}\big);\qquad L_{eb(2)}=\frac{m_{e}}{70m_{b}}\big(-28\Omega_{12}-21\Omega_{22}+6\Omega_{23}+70\big);

\displaystyle{M}_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{23};\qquad{M}_{eb(2)}=\frac{28m_{e}}{5m_{b}}(\Omega_{12}-\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23});

\displaystyle N_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24};

\displaystyle N_{eb(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{14m_{e}^{2}}{5m_{b}^{2}}\Big(-\Omega_{12}+\frac{16}{49}\Omega_{13}-\frac{11}{14}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{14}\Omega_{23}-\frac{3}{49}\Omega_{24}+\frac{6}{49}\Omega_{33}+\frac{10}{7}\Big).

(217)

Note that the expansions with the small
m_{e}/m_{b}
can be easily questioned, because the Chapman-Cowling
integrals
\Omega_{l,j}
are technically undertermined at this stage and can be possibly large.
In such a case, the only correct approach is to solve a fully coupled system
for two species (see for example the Section 8.8, p. 38 “Precision of
m_{e}/m_{i}
expansions” in Part 1, or Appendix N there). Nevertheless, one can be
guided by the electron equations of Part 1, and perform verification for the particular cases of interest a posteriori.

The evolution equations for the stress-tensors read

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\big)^{S}+p_{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{e}=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime};

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{e}=\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime},

(218)

where the collisional contributions become

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{ee}\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}+\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\Big)

\displaystyle-\nu_{eb}\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\nu_{eb}\big(\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\Big);

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}-\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}-7\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\Big)

(219)

\displaystyle-\nu_{eb}\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{23}\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}-\nu_{eb}\big(-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}-7\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\Big),

which can be further re-arranged into

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{33}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}\big)+\nu_{eb}\big(\frac{27}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big]\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\Big[\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\big)+\nu_{eb}\big(\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big]\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{77}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{24}\big)+\nu_{eb}\big(\frac{27}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big]\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}

(220)

\displaystyle+\Big[-\nu_{ee}\big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\big)+\nu_{eb}\big(\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big]\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}.

As a quick double-check, prescribing the Coulomb collisions (with
\Omega_{22}=2
;
\Omega_{23}=4
;
\Omega_{24}=12
) recovers eq. (79) of Part 1.
The quasi-static solution with the general Chapman-Cowling integrals entering the (
220
) can be slightly long to write down
and to clearly understand the solution, it is beneficial to introduce the following notation

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{eb}V_{1}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}+\nu_{eb}V_{2}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{Q}}}^{(4)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{eb}V_{3}\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)}+\nu_{eb}V_{4}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e},

(221)

with the coefficients

\displaystyle V_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big(\frac{33}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}\big)+\big(\frac{27}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle V_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\big)+\big(\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{23}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle V_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big(\frac{77}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{24}\big)+\big(\frac{27}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle V_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[-\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\big)+\big(\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\big)\Big].

(222)

The quasi-static solution of (
218
) and (
221
) then yields the stress-tensors in the usual form

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta_{0}^{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{0}-\eta_{1}^{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{1}-\eta_{2}^{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{2}+\eta_{3}^{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{3}+\eta_{4}^{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{4};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big[-\eta_{0}^{e(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{0}-\eta_{1}^{e(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{1}-\eta_{2}^{e(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{2}+\eta_{3}^{e(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{3}+\eta_{4}^{e(4)}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{4}\Big],

(223)

with the (2-Hermite) “electron” viscosities

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{(7V_{2}-V_{4})}{(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}(V_{1}-7V_{2})+(7V_{2}-V_{4})(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{3}+x(7V_{1}V_{2}-V_{2}V_{3}-7V_{2}V_{4}+V_{4}^{2})\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}(V_{1}^{2}-2V_{2}V_{3}+V_{4}^{2})+(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})^{2},

(224)

and the “electron” viscosities of the stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{(7V_{1}-V_{3})}{(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}(V_{3}-7V_{4})+(7V_{1}-V_{3})(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[7x^{3}+x(7V_{1}^{2}-V_{1}V_{3}-7V_{2}V_{3}+V_{3}V_{4})\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}(V_{1}^{2}-2V_{2}V_{3}+V_{4}^{2})+(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})^{2},

(225)

where the
x=\Omega_{e}/\nu_{eb}
and
\eta_{1}^{e}(x)=\eta_{2}^{e}(2x)
;
\eta_{3}^{e}(x)=\eta_{4}^{e}(2x)
.
Results (
224
)-(
225
) together with the
coefficients (
222
) fully specify the final stress-tensors. Nevertheless, one might have a hope that some coefficients simplify (some indeed do and some
do not), so for clarity, for the usual stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}
we also provide the fully explicit solution, where the parallel “electron” viscosity reads

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta^{*}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{43}{40}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle\equiv(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})=\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{33}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}+\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{129}{200}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{9}{50}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{9}{70}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{18}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)+\frac{18}{175}\big(\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big),

(226)

and the perpendicular viscosities and gyroviscosities are given by

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1\Big)+\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{43}{40}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big]\Delta^{*}\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}x}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}+\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\Big[\frac{2353}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{33}{40}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{81}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big[\frac{18}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}-\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}-\frac{114}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{144}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{231}{40}\Omega_{22}^{2}+\frac{96}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{567}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{144}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{54}{35}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{18}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{72}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{36}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big\{\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\Big[\frac{3433}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{201}{200}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{99}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(227)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big[\frac{1551}{200}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{132}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{162}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{96}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{18}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{729}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{162}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{288}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{18}{25}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{72}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{36}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big\}+\Delta^{*2}.

Result (
226
) represents the parallel electron viscosity of
[
11
]
, here valid for a general class of collisional
processes describable by the Boltzmann operator. Note that we assumed the same collisional process for the “e-e” and “e-b” collisions, and
if these processes are different, the results will become more complicated.

Prescribing the Coulomb collisions in (
226
)-(
227
) (with
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
and
\Omega_{22}=2
;
\Omega_{23}=4
;
\Omega_{24}=12
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\,\frac{\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(41/28)+(51/35)}{[\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}(267/175)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(129/50)+(144/175)]};

(228)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}\frac{6}{5}\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1\Big)+\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{41}{28}+\frac{51}{35}\Big]\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{267}{175}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{129}{50}+\frac{144}{175}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}x}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}+\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{46561}{19600}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{12723}{2450}+\frac{747}{245}\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{79321}{19600}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{22047}{2450}+\frac{6633}{1225}\Big]+\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{267}{175}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{129}{50}+\frac{144}{175}\Big]^{2},

where one needs to use the ratio of the collisional frequencies (
214
),
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/(Z_{b}\sqrt{2})
.
The result (
228
) can be re-arranged into the eq. (82) in
[
43
]
and
the result (
228
) represents the Braginskii electron viscosity for any ion charge
Z_{i}
.

Here we note that even though
[
11
]
provides the electron viscosity only for the ion charge
Z_{i}=1
in his eq. (4.45)
(where the reported parallel electron viscosity 0.733 has a tiny rounding error and should be 0.731 instead), electron viscosities for
any
Z_{i}
can be obtained from eq. (4.15) in his technical paper
[
10
]
, by using the collisional matrices (A.14)
(where the first matrix has to be divided by
Z_{i}\sqrt{2}
). In fact, one can then obtain his model in a fully analytic form, for any
Z_{i}
,
and also verify that the 0.731 is the correct 2-Hermite result. As a side note, the rounding error is of course irrelevant,
but one might get very curious about it, because
from the work of
[
51
]
, the 3-Hermite approximation indeed yields 0.733, so one might be wondering, if Braginskii used the 3-Hermite approximation
for the parallel electron viscosity. However, the perpendicular viscosities and gyroviscosities in eq. (4.45) of
[
11
]
are provided in the
2-Hermite approximation and the collisional matrices for viscosities (A.14) in
[
10
]
, do not go beyond the 2-Hermite approximation. Obviously, this is
only a rounding error, likely originating in eq. (4.16) of the last reference, as a result of
8.50/11.6=0.733
, which was evaluated several years later,
when writing the review paper.

It is interesting to evaluate the general results (
226
)-(
227
) for other collisional forces
and for example, prescribing the hard spheres (with
\Omega_{22}=2
;
\Omega_{23}=8
;
\Omega_{24}=40
, the parallel viscosity is fully meaningfull) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\,\frac{\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(41/28)+(51/35)}{[\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}(303/175)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(1191/350)+(288/175)]};

(229)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}\frac{6}{5}\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1\Big)+\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{41}{28}+\frac{51}{35}\Big]\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{303}{175}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{1191}{350}+\frac{288}{175}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}x}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big\{x^{2}+\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{42529}{19600}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{10707}{2450}+\frac{2727}{1225}\Big\};

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{71257}{19600}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{3603}{490}+\frac{4617}{1225}\Big]+\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\frac{303}{175}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{1191}{350}+\frac{288}{175}\Big]^{2},

where the ratio of the collisional frequencies is given by (
215
). Note the perhaps surprising numerical similarities between the
(
228
) and (
229
) and the parallel viscosities can be also written as

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{1025\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1020}{[1068\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+1806\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+576]}\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}};

(230)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{1025\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1020}{[1212\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+2382\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1152]}\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}.

(231)

As a quick simple double-check, one can recover the self-collisional results by considering the limit of large
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\gg 1
.

VII.1.1
“Electron” viscosities for inverse power-law force

For the inverse power-law force, the V-coefficients (
222
) are evaluated as

\displaystyle V_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\,\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{3(3\nu-5)}{20(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(3\nu+1)+2\nu+6\Big];

\displaystyle V_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{3(3\nu-5)(\nu-5)}{140(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+2\Big];

\displaystyle V_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{40(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(67\nu^{2}-302\nu+283)+48(2\nu-3)(\nu-5)\Big];

\displaystyle V_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{280(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(247\nu^{2}-710\nu+511)+48(2\nu-3)(3\nu-7)\Big],

(232)

which fully define all of the viscosities (
224
)-(
225
). Nevertheless, one might have a hope that some coefficients simplify and for example

\displaystyle 7V_{2}-V_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{280(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(205\nu^{2}-458\nu+301)+204\nu^{2}-600\nu+588\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4}=\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{3(3\nu-5)^{2}}{1400(\nu-1)^{4}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}2(\nu-1)(101\nu-113)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(397\nu^{2}-938\nu+589)+96(\nu-1)(2\nu-3)\Big];

\displaystyle V_{1}-7V_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{3(3\nu-5)}{5(\nu-1)}\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1\Big);

\displaystyle 7V_{1}V_{2}-V_{2}V_{3}-7V_{2}V_{4}+V_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}}{78400(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}(42529\nu^{4}-193828\nu^{3}+356358\nu^{2}-305956\nu

\displaystyle+103201)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(85656\nu^{4}-457056\nu^{3}+1006224\nu^{2}-1020768\nu+404376)

\displaystyle+43632\nu^{4}-268992\nu^{3}+692640\nu^{2}-826560\nu+396144\Big];

\displaystyle V_{1}^{2}-2V_{2}V_{3}+V_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}}{78400(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}(71257\nu^{4}-312772\nu^{3}+548886\nu^{2}-449092\nu

(233)

\displaystyle+144025)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(144120\nu^{4}-707040\nu^{3}+1437648\nu^{2}-1367520\nu+511224)

\displaystyle+73872\nu^{4}-406080\nu^{3}+954720\nu^{2}-1060416\nu+474768\Big],

and these expressions enter the viscosities (
224
). Obviously, the factorizations simplify the final results only slightly and
if one wants a viscosity for a given
\nu
, it seems easier to first calculate the V-coefficients (
232
) for that given
\nu
and
calculate the viscosities (
224
) only afterwards. Nevertheless, for example the parallel viscosity reads

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\frac{5(\nu-1)}{3(3\nu-5)}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(205\nu^{2}-458\nu+301)+204\nu^{2}-600\nu+588\Big]

(234)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}2(\nu-1)(101\nu-113)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}(397\nu^{2}-938\nu+589)+96(\nu-1)(2\nu-3)\Big]^{-1}.

The result (
234
) represents the Braginskii parallel “electron” viscosity for any force
K_{ab}/r^{\nu}

and the cases (
230
)-(
231
) are recovered by prescribing
\nu=2
and
\nu\to\infty
. The self-collisional limit of (
234
) recovers the parallel
viscosity (
180
) and the same can be seen for the
\eta_{2}^{e}
and
\eta_{4}^{e}
given by (
233
).

VII.1.2
Braginskii (
\nu=2
) electron viscosities for moderately-coupled plasmas

Evaluation of the previous equations for
\nu=2
yields a generalization of the Braginskii electron viscosities for moderately-coupled plasmas

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{1}(2)}{A_{2}(2)}\frac{5(205\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+204)}{3[178\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+301\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+96]};

(235)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\frac{3}{5}\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1\Big)+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{3}\frac{3}{392000}\Big(205\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+204\Big)\Big(178\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+301\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+96\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{3}+x\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}\frac{1}{78400}\Big(46561\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+101784\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+59760\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{2}\frac{1}{78400}\Big(79321\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+176376\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+106128\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}\Big)^{4}\Big[\frac{3}{1400}\Big(178\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+301\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+96\Big)\Big]^{2},

where the
x=\Omega_{e}/\nu_{eb}
, the
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/(Z_{b}\sqrt{2})
and the coefficients
A_{l}(2)
are given by (
35
)-(
36
).
The limit of weakly-coupled plasmas
(\ln\Lambda\gg 1)
is obtained by
A_{2}(2)/A_{1}(2)=2
, recovering eq. (82) of Part 1 (the last
Z_{i}
there is missing the index “i”),
or here eq. (
228
).

VII.1.3
Reduction into 1-Hermite approximation

In the simplified 1-Hermite approximation, the “electron” stress-tensor evolves according to

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\big)^{S}+p_{e}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{e}=-\nu_{eb}(1+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{e}^{(2)},

(236)

which in the quasi-static approximation yields the 1-Hermite viscosities

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{5}{3\Omega_{22}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})};\qquad\big[\eta_{2}^{e}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,(1+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22};

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{4}^{e}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\Delta}\,x;\qquad\Delta=x^{2}+\Big((1+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big)^{2}.

(237)

In the limit of strong magnetic field (
x\gg 1
), the 2-Hermite perpendicular viscosities and gyroviscosites (
227
) converge to
the 1-Hermite approximation

\textrm{Strong B-field}:\qquad\eta_{2}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}\nu_{eb}}{\Omega_{e}^{2}}\,\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}(1+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}});\qquad\eta_{4}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{\Omega_{e}},

(238)

and only the parallel viscosities
\eta_{0}^{e}
remain different. For the inverse power-law force, the 1-Hermite parallel viscosity reads

\textrm{Inverse power}:\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\frac{5(\nu-1)}{3(3\nu-5)}\frac{1}{(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1)},

(239)

and the 2-Hermite result is given by (
234
).

VII.1.4
Improvement of the 2-Hermite approximation

For a general collisional process, the improvement of the 2-Hermite approximation for the parallel viscosity can be written as

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big\{1+3(7\Omega_{22}-2\Omega_{23})^{2}\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+2\big)^{2}\Big[\Big(154\Omega_{22}^{2}+12\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-12\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}

(240)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(301\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}(60\Omega_{24}-84\Omega_{23})-48\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+48\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-48\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big]^{-1}\Big\},

which for the inverse power-law force becomes

\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{3(\nu-5)^{2}(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+2)^{2}}{2(\nu-1)(101\nu-113)(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})^{2}+(397\nu^{2}-938\nu+589)\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+96(\nu-1)(2\nu-3)}\Big),

(241)

and for the two particular cases

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{27(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+2)^{2}}{178(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})^{2}+301\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+96}\Big);

(242)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{3(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+2)^{2}}{202(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}})^{2}+397\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+192}\Big).

(243)

Note that for the Maxwell molecules (
\nu=5
) the improvement of (
241
) is zero and in the self-collisional limit
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\gg 1
,
one recovers results (
185
)-(
186
). The above results are useful, however, to get an idea about the improvement, one needs some concrete numbers.
First, we consider the particular case with the ratio of collisional frequences

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\qquad\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.731\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}};\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\big(1+0.497\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.507\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}};\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\big(1+0.0383\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions \& Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.488\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}},

(244)

where the 0.731 is the Braginskii parallel electron viscosity (for the
Z_{i}=1
).
The result (
244
) shows that while for the Coulomb collisions the parallel electron viscosity is improved by almost 50% by the 2-Hermite approximation,
for the hard spheres the improvement is less than 4%.

As a second example, we consider the Lorentz limit (which is the most extreme case), with the ratio of collisional frequencies

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\ll 1;\qquad\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(85/48)}_{1.77}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}};\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\underbrace{(9/8)}_{1.125}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(85/96)}_{0.885}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}};\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{2}=\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\underbrace{(1/16)}_{0.0625}\Big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions \& Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\eta_{0}^{e}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(5/6)}_{0.833}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}},

(245)

implying that while for the Coulomb collisions the correction of the 2-Hermite approximation is over 110%, for the hard spheres it is
only 6%.

VII.2
Heat fluxes
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
,
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}
and momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{e}

For
m_{e}\ll m_{b}
, the mass-ratio coefficients for small temperature differences (
128
)-(
132
) simplify into

\displaystyle D_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13};\qquad D_{eb(2)}=-\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{22}+3;

\displaystyle U_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Omega_{12};\qquad U_{eb(2)}=+4\Omega_{13};

\displaystyle E_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{14};\qquad E_{eb(2)}=\frac{m_{e}}{m_{b}}\Big(-\frac{3}{4}+\frac{17}{40}\Omega_{12}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{13}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{14}+\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{23}\Big);

\displaystyle F_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\Omega_{13}+\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{14};\qquad F_{eb(2)}=-\frac{168}{5}\Omega_{12}+4\Omega_{13}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{32}{5}\Omega_{23};

\displaystyle G_{eb(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{13}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15};

\displaystyle G_{eb(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{e}}{m_{b}}\frac{7}{2}\Big(-\frac{32}{245}\Omega_{33}+\frac{12}{5}\Omega_{12}-\frac{251}{245}\Omega_{13}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{245}\Omega_{15}+\frac{32}{35}\Omega_{22}-\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}+\frac{32}{245}\Omega_{24}-\frac{10}{7}\Big).

The collisional right-hand-sides become

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{5}\nu_{ee}\Omega_{22}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big)

(246)

\displaystyle+\nu_{eb}\Big\{+\Big(\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\Omega_{12}p_{e}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{e})

\displaystyle+\Big(-\frac{1}{8}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{14}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big)\Big\}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bm{R}_{e};

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{28}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big)\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}-\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-28\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big)

(247)

\displaystyle+\nu_{eb}\Big\{+\Big(4\Omega_{13}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{14}\Big)\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+4\Omega_{13}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{e})

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{13}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15}\Big)\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-28\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big)\Big\}-35\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}^{2}}\bm{R}_{e},

with the momentum exchange rates

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu_{eb}\Big\{\rho_{e}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{e})+\Big(1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Big)\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}-\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big)\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-28\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big)\Big\};

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu_{eb}\Big\{\rho_{e}(\bm{u}_{b}-\bm{u}_{e})+\Big(\frac{9}{2}-\frac{16}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big)\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}-\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big)\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}\Big\},

(248)

which can be re-arranged into

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{9}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Big)+\nu_{eb}\Big(\frac{45}{4}-\frac{25}{2}\Omega_{12}+\frac{21}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}\Big)\Big]\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}

(249)

\displaystyle+\Big[\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)+\nu_{eb}\Big(\frac{5}{16}-\frac{3}{8}\Omega_{12}+\frac{19}{140}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{14}\Big)\Big]\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{5}{2}-\Omega_{12}\Big)\nu_{eb}p_{e}(\bm{u}_{e}-\bm{u}_{b});

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+14\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\nu_{eb}\Big(\frac{315}{2}-112\Omega_{12}-4\Omega_{13}+\frac{64}{5}\Omega_{14}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{15}\Big)\Big]\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}

(250)

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{ee}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)+\nu_{eb}\Big(-\frac{35}{8}+\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15}\Big)\Big]\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}

\displaystyle+\big(35-4\Omega_{13}\big)\nu_{eb}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}(\bm{u}_{e}-\bm{u}_{b}).

As a quick double-check, prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers eqs. (48)-(50) of
[
43
]
,
where we have used the notation
\delta\bm{u}=\bm{u}_{e}-\bm{u}_{b}
(analogous to the Braginskii notation
\bm{u}=\bm{u}_{e}-\bm{u}_{i}
).

The collisional contributions (
249
)-(
250
) enter the right-hand-side of the evolution equations for the heat flux vectors

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\frac{5}{2}p_{e}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime};

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}+70\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}.

(251)

To write the general solution in the quasi-static approximation, it is useful to introduce notation

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(3)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu_{eb}\Big[-B_{1}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+B_{2}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}+B_{5}p_{e}\delta\bm{u}\Big];

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{Q}}^{(5)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu_{eb}\Big[-B_{3}\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}-B_{4}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}+B_{6}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\delta\bm{u}\Big];

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu_{eb}\Big[-\rho_{e}\delta\bm{u}+B_{7}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}-B_{8}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}\Big],

(252)

with the following B-coefficients
(big B as Braginskii, who was the first to figure out all of the electron transport coefficents in the presence of magnetic field)

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{9}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Big)+\Big(\frac{45}{4}-\frac{25}{2}\Omega_{12}+\frac{21}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)+\Big(\frac{5}{16}-\frac{3}{8}\Omega_{12}+\frac{19}{140}\Omega_{13}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{14}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+14\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\Big(\frac{315}{2}-112\Omega_{12}-4\Omega_{13}+\frac{64}{5}\Omega_{14}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{15}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle B_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)+\Big(-\frac{35}{8}+\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{12}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle B_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{5}{2}-\Omega_{12}\Big);\qquad B_{6}=\big(35-4\Omega_{13}\big);

\displaystyle B_{7}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}+28B_{8}=\Big(\frac{9}{2}-\frac{16}{5}\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big);\qquad B_{8}=\Big(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{10}\Omega_{12}+\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{13}\Big).

(253)

The collisional contributions (
252
)-(
253
) enter the right-hand-sides of the evolution equations (
251
). Note that for
the momentum exchange rates, the coefficients are related to the previously defined
B_{5}=(5/2)V_{ab(0)}
;
B_{7}=V_{eb(1)}
and
B_{8}=V_{ab(3)}
, but
here to write down the solutions, we find it better to use the B-designation.

VII.2.1
Braginskii form for
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
(through Chapman-Cowling integrals)

In the quasi-static approximation, the heat flux is split into the thermal and frictional part
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}=\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}+\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}
, where
by using the
[
11
]
notation for the transport coefficients

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e};

(254)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\gamma_{0};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle};\qquad\triangle=x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0};

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{0}p_{e}\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+p_{e}\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle}-p_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle}.

(255)

The thermal heat conductivities (
254
) are given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5(28B_{2}+B_{4})}{2(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{5}{2}B_{1}-70B_{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{5}{2}(28B_{2}+B_{4})(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{B_{2}}{2}(140B_{1}-5B_{3}+140B_{4})+\frac{5}{2}B_{4}^{2};

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{1}^{2}-2B_{2}B_{3}+B_{4}^{2};\qquad\delta_{0}=(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})^{2},

(256)

where the B-coefficients (
253
) contain the Chapman-Cowling integrals.
The frictional heat flux (
255
) is given by

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5}}{B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3}};\qquad\beta_{1}^{\prime}=B_{1}B_{5}-B_{2}B_{6};\qquad\beta_{0}^{\prime}=(B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5})(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{5};\qquad\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}=B_{2}(B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5}+B_{4}B_{6})+B_{4}^{2}B_{5},

(257)

with the same denomintor
\triangle
.

VII.2.2
Solution for
\vec{\bm{X}}_{e}^{(5)}
(through Chapman-Cowling integrals)

Analogously to
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
, one writes the solution for the heat flux
\vec{\bm{X}}_{e}^{(5)}
split into the
thermal and frictional parts

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{X}}_{e}^{(5)T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big[-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e(5)}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\Big];

(258)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\gamma_{0}^{(5)};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}}{\triangle};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e(5)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle};

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{X}}_{e}^{(5)u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\Big[\beta_{0}^{(5)}\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u}\Big],

(259)

where the
\triangle
is the same as in (
254
). The thermal transport coefficients read

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5(28B_{1}-B_{3})}{2(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2}B_{3}+70B_{4};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2}(28B_{1}-B_{3})(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 70;\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}=-\,\frac{B_{3}}{2}(5B_{1}+140B_{2}+5B_{4})+70B_{1}^{2},

(260)

and the frictional coefficients read

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5}}{B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3}};\qquad\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}=B_{3}B_{5}+B_{4}B_{6};\qquad\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}=(B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5})(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{6};\qquad\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}=-\,B_{3}(B_{1}B_{5}+B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5})+B_{1}^{2}B_{6},

(261)

where the B-coefficients are given by (
253
).

VII.2.3
Momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{e}
(through Chapman-Cowling integrals)

To finish the task of expressing all of the Braginskii transport coefficients through the Chapman-Cowling integrals,
one needs to substitute the
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}
solutions given above into the momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{e}
(
252
)
and write the results in the Braginskii form, by also spllitting the
\bm{R}_{e}
into the
frictional and thermal parts

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{e}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha_{0}\rho_{e}\nu_{eb}\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}-\rho_{e}\nu_{eb}\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}\Big(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\alpha_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle}\Big)-\rho_{e}\nu_{eb}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u}\frac{\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle};

(262)

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{e}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta_{0}n_{e}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-n_{e}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle}+n_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle}.

(263)

The coefficients for the frictional part (
262
) are then obtained by

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-B_{7}\beta_{0}+B_{8}\beta_{0}^{(5)};\qquad\alpha_{1}^{\prime}=B_{7}\beta_{1}^{\prime}-B_{8}\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}};

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}\beta_{0}^{\prime}-B_{8}\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}};\qquad\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}=B_{7}\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}-B_{8}\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}};\qquad\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}=B_{7}\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}-B_{8}\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}},

(264)

and more explicitly as

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-B_{7}\frac{(B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5})}{(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})}+B_{8}\frac{(B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5})}{(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})};

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}(B_{1}B_{5}-B_{2}B_{6})-B_{8}(B_{3}B_{5}+B_{4}B_{6});

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[B_{7}(B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5})-B_{8}(B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5})\big](B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}B_{5}-B_{8}B_{6};

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}\big[B_{2}(B_{1}B_{6}-B_{3}B_{5}+B_{4}B_{6})+B_{4}^{2}B_{5}\big]-B_{8}\big[-\,B_{3}(B_{1}B_{5}+B_{2}B_{6}+B_{4}B_{5})+B_{1}^{2}B_{6}\big],

(265)

where the B-coefficients are given by (
253
).

The coefficients for the thermal part (
263
) (often called the thermal force)
were already written with the
\beta
-coefficients of the frictional heat flux (
255
), because
the system satisfies the Onsager symmetry, and it can be verified that the following relations are indeed true

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}\gamma_{0}-B_{8}\gamma_{0}^{(5)};\qquad\beta_{1}^{\prime}=B_{7}\gamma_{1}^{\prime}-B_{8}\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}};

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{7}\gamma_{0}^{\prime}-B_{8}\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}};\qquad\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}=B_{7}\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}-B_{8}\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}};\qquad\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}=B_{7}\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}-B_{8}\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}.

(266)

The Onsager symmetry - i.e. that the transport coefficients
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{0}^{\prime},\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime},\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
of the fricional heat flux (
255
)
are identical to the transport coefficients of the thermal force (
263
), is actualy a useful double-check that our calculations are correct.

VII.2.4
Re-arranged Braginskii coefficients (through Chapman-Cowling integrals)

At this stage, all of the Braginskii transport coefficients (the
\gamma
-coefficients for the thermal heat flux
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}
(
256
),
the
\beta
-coefficients for the frictional heat flux
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}
(
257
),
as well as the
\alpha
-coefficients for the momentum exchange rates (
265
)), were already expressed through
the Chapman-Cowling integrals fully explicitly, by using the B-coefficients (
253
). One might naturally wonder, if a re-grouping of
these expressions might bring some simplifications, and here we re-group the results with respect to
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}
.
For some coefficients, this re-grouping is partially beneficial, however for a few cases, the re-grouping yields expressions which are too long.

Starting with the parallel heat conductivity, the re-grouping yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{2\Delta^{*}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{11}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\frac{35}{8}-7\Omega_{12}+\frac{19}{5}\Omega_{13}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{15}\Big]\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}m_{e}};

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle\equiv(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})=\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}\Big(\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{24}

\displaystyle+\frac{11}{25}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{15}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{14}+\frac{48}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\Omega_{22}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}+\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{15}-\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{14}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{19}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{15}+\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{13}-\Omega_{14}.

(267)

The result (
267
) can be viewed as the parallel electron thermal conductivity of
[
11
]
,
here generalized to a form valid for any lightweight particles
m_{e}\ll m_{b}
that
the classical Boltzmann operator can describe. Note that we assumed the same collisional process for the “e-e” and “e-b” collisions,
and one could derive a more general result, where these two processes are different.
Prescribing the Coulomb collisions and the hard spheres (with the
\Omega_{l,j}
given by (
50
)-(
51
)) yields parallel thermal conductivities

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{25}{4}\frac{(360\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+433)}{(576\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+1208\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+217)}\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}m_{e}};

(268)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

\displaystyle=\frac{25}{4}\frac{(360\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+433)}{(704\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+1944\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1275)}\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}m_{e}},

(269)

where the collisional frequencies are given by (
214
)-(
215
). As a partial double-check, taking the results (
267
)-(
269
)
and considering the self-collisional limit
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}\gg 1
,
recovers the self-collisional results (
198
)-(
201
).
Prescribing
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/\sqrt{2}
then recovers the famous Braginskii value of
\gamma_{0}=3.1616
(valid for the ion charge
Z_{i}=1
) and for the
hard spheres
\gamma_{0}=1.4316
, also compared in (
301
) and together with other coefficients in Table
1
.

Note that the re-grouping with
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}
did not simlify the final result (
267
) that much, and one might as well use the formulation
(
256
) through the B-coefficients. Nevertheless, since the denominator
\Delta^{*}
is already written down, the other two parallel coefficients are

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta^{*}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{5}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{23}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}-\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}+\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}\Big)

(270)

\displaystyle+\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{13}+\frac{3}{2}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}-\frac{19}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}-\frac{3}{2}\Omega_{14}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}-\frac{7}{2}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{15}-\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}\Big];

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{\Delta^{*}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}-\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{24}+\frac{11}{25}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}^{2}\Omega_{22}

(271)

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}^{2}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\Omega_{22}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}^{3}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{15}-\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}

\displaystyle+\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}-\frac{19}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{15}+\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{13}-\Omega_{14}\Big].

For the thermal heat flux, the rest of the Braginskii
\gamma
-coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Omega_{22}+\frac{25}{4}-5\Omega_{12}+\Omega_{13};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\gamma_{0}\Delta^{*2};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{5}{2};

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{149}{40}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{13}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{22}+\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{11}{35}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{2}{245}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{4}{245}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{15}-2\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-49\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{38}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{24}+\frac{247}{10}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-\frac{24}{5}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{22}+\frac{11}{35}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{15}

\displaystyle\quad+17\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}+\frac{12}{7}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{14}-\frac{304}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}-\frac{8}{35}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{15}+\frac{5}{4}\Omega_{24}+\frac{525}{16}\Omega_{22}-\frac{45}{4}\Omega_{23}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{9625}{128}-\frac{875}{4}\Omega_{12}+\frac{855}{8}\Omega_{13}-20\Omega_{14}+\frac{1295}{8}\Omega_{12}^{2}-\frac{323}{2}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+31\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}+\frac{1444}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{114}{7}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}+\frac{58}{35}\Omega_{14}^{2}+\frac{5}{4}\Omega_{15}-2\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}+\frac{38}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{15}-\frac{8}{35}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{15}+\frac{2}{245}\Omega_{15}^{2},

together with

\displaystyle\delta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{*2};

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

(273)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{8}{1225}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{15}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{127}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{76}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{24}+\frac{293}{25}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-\frac{52}{25}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{22}+\frac{22}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{15}

\displaystyle+\frac{3025}{64}+\frac{4}{1225}\Omega_{15}^{2}+\frac{169}{2}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{68}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}-\frac{388}{5}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}-\frac{1216}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}

\displaystyle+\frac{76}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{15}+\frac{24}{35}\Omega_{14}^{2}+\frac{3277}{175}\Omega_{13}^{2}-\frac{16}{175}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{15}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{15}-\frac{495}{4}\Omega_{12}+\frac{217}{4}\Omega_{13}-9\Omega_{14}.

For the frictional heat flux, the magnetized Braginskii

-coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\prime}

(274)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{35}{2}\Omega_{22}-6\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-18\Omega_{23}+\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}+14\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+6\Omega_{24}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{8}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{24}\Big)

where the last expression for

was re-arranged only partially (otherwise further re-grouping with

makes this expression too long).
Again, the heat flux expressions above did not simplify much and the original formulation by first calculating the B-coefficients (
253
)
for a given collisional process and then calculating the Braginskii coefficients (

)-(
257
), seems to be more user-friendly.

For the momentum exchange rates, the first three
\alpha
-coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{18}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{127}{10}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{1}{4}\Omega_{24}+\frac{149}{16}\Omega_{22}-\frac{11}{4}\Omega_{23}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{3025}{64}-\frac{678}{175}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}^{2}-\frac{64}{25}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{15}+\frac{516}{25}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}^{2}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{13}^{2}\Omega_{14}

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{24}+\frac{11}{25}\Omega_{12}^{2}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}^{2}\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}-\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\Omega_{22}\Big)
