\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{55}{8}-9\Omega_{12}+\Omega_{13}+\frac{16}{5}\Omega_{12}^{2}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}.

(276)

The last fourth coefficient
\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}
given by (
265
) becomes way too long after the re-grouping,
and calculation through the B-coefficients (
253
) is much easier.

VII.2.5
Braginskii coefficients for inverse power-law force

For the inverse power law force
F_{ab}=\pm|K_{ab}|/r^{\nu}
, the B-coefficients (
253
) become

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(\nu+3)}{5(\nu-1)^{2}}+\frac{(3\nu^{3}+67\nu^{2}-191\nu+185)}{20(\nu-1)^{3}};

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(\nu-5)}{140(\nu-1)^{2}}-\frac{(\nu-5)(23\nu^{2}-62\nu+55)}{560(\nu-1)^{3}};

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{2(3\nu-5)(29\nu^{2}-122\nu+109)}{5(\nu-1)^{3}}-\frac{(\nu-5)(15\nu-19)(23\nu^{2}-62\nu+55)}{10(\nu-1)^{4}};

\displaystyle B_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(59\nu^{2}-190\nu+147)}{70(\nu-1)^{3}}+\frac{(755\nu^{4}-5396\nu^{3}+13898\nu^{2}-16036\nu+7035)}{280(\nu-1)^{4}};

\displaystyle B_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{(\nu-5)}{2(\nu-1)};\qquad B_{6}=-\,\frac{(\nu-5)(13\nu-17)}{(\nu-1)^{2}},

(277)

and the Braginskii
\gamma
-coefficients are obtained by (
256
),
the
\beta
-coefficients by (
257
) and the
\alpha
-coefficients by (
265
).
The values of
A_{l}(\nu)
are given in Table
2
and the
ratios of collisional frequencies
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}
are given by (
216
). Note that for the Maxwell molecules (
\nu=5
), the coefficients

B_{5}
and
B_{6}
become zero and thus all of the
\beta
-coefficients (
257
) and (
261
) become zero as well,
eliminating the frictional heat fluxes
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)u}_{e}
. Also, for the Maxwell molecules the coefficient
\alpha_{0}=1
and all of the other
\alpha
-coefficients
(
265
) become zero, yielding a simple (isotropic) momentum exchange rates
\bm{R}_{e}=-\rho_{e}\nu_{eb}\delta\bm{u}
.

Expressing the transport coefficients explicitly, the coefficient of the parallel thermal conductivity reads

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{2\Delta^{*}}\frac{1}{(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(45\nu^{2}-106\nu+77)}{70}+\frac{(433\nu^{4}-2596\nu^{3}+6310\nu^{2}-7076\nu+3185)}{280(\nu-1)}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{(3\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{4(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)}{175}+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(243\nu^{3}-1077\nu^{2}+1745\nu-975)}{175(\nu-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(425\nu^{4}-2516\nu^{3}+6118\nu^{2}-7156\nu+3385)}{700(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big],

(278)

where the
(\nu-1)^{3}
cancells out, but we kept this form to be able to use the
\Delta^{*}
in the expressions below.
The electron heat flux of
[
11
]
is obtained by prescribing
\nu=2
.
The other two parallel coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta^{*}}\frac{(\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)^{3}}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{70}\Big[-\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}(23\nu-31)-\frac{(19\nu^{2}-62\nu+59)}{(\nu-1)}\Big];

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}(3\nu-5)}{175(\nu-1)^{4}\Delta^{*}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}2(6\nu-7)+\frac{(41\nu^{2}-122\nu+97)}{4(\nu-1)}\Big].

(279)

The rest of coefficients for the thermal heat flux read

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\nu-1)}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}(3\nu-5)+\frac{(13\nu^{2}-42\nu+45)}{4(\nu-1)}\Big];\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\gamma_{0}\Delta^{*2};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{5}{2};

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(2053\nu^{4}-9876\nu^{3}+19454\nu^{2}-18004\nu+6629)}{1960}

(280)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(20129\nu^{6}-172182\nu^{5}+644463\nu^{4}-1324148\nu^{3}+1570351\nu^{2}-1018262\nu+283745)}{3920(\nu-1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{31360(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big(202301\nu^{8}-2505736\nu^{7}+14021772\nu^{6}-45784056\nu^{5}+95060494\nu^{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-128340920\nu^{3}+110080076\nu^{2}-54930120\nu+12261725\Big)\Big],

together with

\displaystyle\delta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{*2};

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(2865\nu^{4}-13348\nu^{3}+25446\nu^{2}-22820\nu+8113)}{4900}

(281)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(30965\nu^{6}-255342\nu^{5}+924603\nu^{4}-1840580\nu^{3}+2115035\nu^{2}-1328110\nu+357525)}{9800(\nu-1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{78400(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big(349609\nu^{8}-4149448\nu^{7}+22359612\nu^{6}-70510072\nu^{5}+141608310\nu^{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-185034872\nu^{3}+153575612\nu^{2}-74100040\nu+15966825\Big)\Big].

For the frictional heat flux the coefficients become

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)^{4}}\Big[-\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(27\nu^{2}-54\nu+43)(3\nu-5)}{140}-\,\frac{(341\nu^{4}-1796\nu^{3}+4142\nu^{2}-4516\nu+2085)}{560(\nu-1)}\Big];

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{0}\Delta^{*2};\qquad\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}=-\,\frac{(\nu-5)}{2(\nu-1)};

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)(3\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[-\,\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(3\nu-5)(1063\nu^{3}-4029\nu^{2}+5365\nu-2527)}{4900}

(282)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(21367\nu^{5}-159667\nu^{4}+492358\nu^{3}-776006\nu^{2}+624947\nu-207095)}{19600(\nu-1)}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{(9193\nu^{6}-87790\nu^{5}+359407\nu^{4}-801188\nu^{3}+1025783\nu^{2}-716174\nu+214865)}{19600(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big].

Finally for the momentum exchange rates the coefficients read

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}}{(\nu-1)^{7}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)(773\nu^{4}-2748\nu^{3}+5110\nu^{2}-5196\nu+2317)}{19600}

\displaystyle+\frac{(9337\nu^{6}-65070\nu^{5}+218687\nu^{4}-430500\nu^{3}+518439\nu^{2}-356942\nu+110145)}{78400(\nu-1)}\Big];

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}(3\nu-5)^{2}}{(\nu-1)^{7}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{3}\frac{8(6\nu-7)(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)}{30625}

(283)

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(4269\nu^{4}-24206\nu^{3}+53352\nu^{2}-53178\nu+19955)}{30625(\nu-1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(15063\nu^{5}-109945\nu^{4}+335150\nu^{3}-528858\nu^{2}+429019\nu-141965)}{122500(\nu-1)^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(41\nu^{2}-122\nu+97)(425\nu^{4}-2516\nu^{3}+6118\nu^{2}-7156\nu+3385)}{490000(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big],

together with

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}(29\nu^{2}-50\nu+37)}{280(\nu-1)^{4}};

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\nu-5)^{2}(3\nu-5)^{2}}{(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big[\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\frac{(557\nu^{2}-1482\nu+989)}{12250}

(284)

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(479\nu^{3}-2197\nu^{2}+3517\nu-1927)}{6125(\nu-1)}+\frac{4(53\nu^{4}-347\nu^{3}+885\nu^{2}-1037\nu+478)}{6125(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big].

This concludes the formulation of the model for the inverse force. As previously, only the parallel coefficients
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\gamma_{0}
are
valid for any power-law index
\nu
and unfortunatelly, the magnetized coefficients are valid only for
\nu=2
, nevertheless,
we will use these equations in the next Section
VII.2.6
to obtain the Braginskii model for moderately-coupled plasmas.
To obtain more general results, one needs to consider
coupling between charged particles and neutrals, where the results will of course become more complicated.

The Lorentz approximation is obtained easily from all of the coefficients and for example the parallel heat flux reads

\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\ll 1;\qquad\gamma_{0}=\frac{25}{4}\frac{(\nu-1)(433\nu^{4}-2596\nu^{3}+6310\nu^{2}-7076\nu+3185)}{(3\nu-5)(425\nu^{4}-2516\nu^{3}+6118\nu^{2}-7156\nu+3385)}.

(285)

Fixing the ratio of collisional frequencies into
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/\sqrt{2}
, the Braginskii parameters for various repulsive forces are compared
in Table
1
.

Table 1:
Comparison of Braginskii parallel coefficients for the repulsive force
K_{ab}/r^{\nu}
, where the
ratio of collisional frequencies is
\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb}=1/\sqrt{2}
. For the Coulomb collisions (
\nu=2
), it does not matter that the “e-i” collisions
are attractive, because the numbers are the same. For the other
\nu
cases, both the “e-e” and “e-b” collisions are repulsive.
One can create similar table for attractive forces, by using results of Section
X.4
.

VII.2.6
Braginskii (
\nu=2
) electron coefficients for moderately-coupled plasmas

Here we consider the Coulomb case
\nu=2
and summarize the Braginskii electron coefficients for plasmas, where the Coulomb logarithm
\ln\Lambda
is not necessarily large.
For the brevity of expressions, we use a simple notation

A\equiv\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{ei}}\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)}=\frac{1}{Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}\frac{A_{2}(2)}{A_{1}(2)},

(286)

where as a reminder the
Z_{i}
is the ion charge and

A_{1}(2)=\ln(\Lambda^{2}+1);\qquad A_{2}(2)=2\ln(\Lambda^{2}+1)-2+\frac{2}{\Lambda^{2}+1}.

The parallel electron Braginskii coefficients are then given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}=\frac{4(16+61A+36A^{2})}{217+604A+144A^{2}};\qquad\beta_{0}=\frac{30(11+15A)}{217+604A+144A^{2}};\qquad\gamma_{0}=\frac{25(433+180A)}{4(217+604A+144A^{2})},

(287)

and the perpendicular coefficients read

\displaystyle\delta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{217+604A+144A^{2}}{700}\Big)^{2};\qquad\delta_{1}=\frac{3313}{4900}A^{2}+\frac{41269}{9800}A+\frac{586601}{78400};

(288)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{24741}{19600}A+\frac{363033}{78400};\qquad\alpha_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{9(217+604A+144A^{2})(40A+17)}{490000};

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{477}{280};\qquad\alpha_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{2277}{12250}A^{2}+\frac{1359}{6125}A+\frac{576}{6125};

(289)

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{129}{140}A+\frac{2127}{560};\qquad\beta_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{3(217+604A+144A^{2})(15A+11)}{49000};

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2};\qquad\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1773}{4900}A^{2}+\frac{20133}{19600}A+\frac{17187}{19600};

(290)

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A+\frac{13}{4};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{(217+604A+144A^{2})(180A+433)}{78400};

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{2277}{1960}A^{2}+\frac{25281}{3920}A+\frac{320797}{31360}.

(291)

The above expressions represent the Braginskii electron heat fluxes and momentum exchange rates for moderatelly-coupled plasmas. In the limit when the Coulomb logarithm becomes
large, the coefficient
A=\sqrt{2}/Z_{i}
and one recovers expressions (56)-(60) of
[
43
]
for weakly-coupled plasmas.

VII.2.7
Reduction into 1-Hermite approximation

In the 1-Hermite approximation, the heat flux evolves according to

\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+\frac{5}{2}p_{e}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big)=\nu_{eb}\Big[-B_{1}\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}+B_{5}p_{e}\delta\bm{u}\Big];

(292)

with coefficients

B_{1}=\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{5}{2}-2\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big];\qquad B_{5}=\big(\frac{5}{2}-\Omega_{12}\big).

(293)

As a quick double-check, prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers eqs. (H40)-(H41) of Part 1. The solutions are

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e};

(294)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\frac{5}{2B_{1}}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e}=\frac{5}{2}\frac{B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e}=\frac{5}{2}\frac{x}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}};

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{5}}{B_{1}}p_{e}\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+p_{e}\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}\frac{B_{5}B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}-p_{e}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u}\frac{B_{5}x}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}.

(295)

Note that for both the Coulomb collisions and hard spheres
B_{1}=(4/5)(\nu_{ee}/\nu_{eb})+(13/10)
and the entire thermal heat flux is the same.
The 1-Hermite parallel thermal conductivity reads

\displaystyle\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(25/4)}{(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Omega_{22}+(25/4)-5\Omega_{12}+\Omega_{13})};

\displaystyle\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25}{4}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{(3\nu-5)}{(\nu-1)}+\frac{(13\nu^{2}-42\nu+45)}{4(\nu-1)^{2}}\Big]^{-1},

(296)

where the
dimensionless
\gamma_{0}
coefficient is defined as
\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\gamma_{0}p_{e}/(\nu_{eb}m_{e})
.

VII.2.8
Improvement of the 2-Hermite approximation

For a general collisional process, the improvement of the 2-Hermite approximation for the parallel thermal conductivity reads

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big\{1+\Big((28\Omega_{22}-8\Omega_{23})\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}-210\Omega_{12}+76\Omega_{13}-8\Omega_{14}+175\Big)^{2}

(297)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\Big(448\Omega_{22}^{2}+64\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-64\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)\Big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}+\Big((1232\Omega_{22}+768\Omega_{23}+64\Omega_{24})\Omega_{13}

\displaystyle\qquad+(-2240\Omega_{22}-1120\Omega_{23}-320\Omega_{24})\Omega_{12}+(-448\Omega_{14}+64\Omega_{15}+2800)\Omega_{22}

\displaystyle\qquad-128\Omega_{23}\Omega_{14}+400\Omega_{24}\Big)\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}-1520\Omega_{13}^{2}+(2800\Omega_{12}+320\Omega_{14}+64\Omega_{15}+4900)\Omega_{13}

\displaystyle\qquad-4900\Omega_{12}^{2}+(1120\Omega_{14}-320\Omega_{15})\Omega_{12}-64\Omega_{14}^{2}-2800\Omega_{14}+400\Omega_{15}\Big]^{-1}\Big\},

and for the inverse power-law force

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big\{1+(\nu-5)^{2}\Big(4(\nu-1)(3\nu-5)\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+23\nu^{2}-62\nu+55\Big)

(298)

\displaystyle\times\Big[16(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)(\nu-1)^{2}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big)^{2}+4(\nu-1)(243\nu^{3}-1077\nu^{2}+1745\nu-975)\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}

\displaystyle+425\nu^{4}-2516\nu^{3}+6118\nu^{2}-7156\nu+3385\Big]^{-1}\Big\},

and for the two particular cases

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{9(8\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+23)^{2}}{4\big(576\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+1208\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+217\big)}\Big);

(299)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big]_{1}\Big(1+\frac{(8\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+23)^{2}}{4\big(704\big(\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big)^{2}+1944\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1275\big)}\Big).

(300)

To get a better sense about the improvement of (
299
)-(
300
), it is again useful to consider two particular cases,
where in the first case the ratio of collisional frequencies is fixed as

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\qquad\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25(180\sqrt{2}+433)}{4(505+604\sqrt{2})}=3.1616;\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}=\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}\big(1+1.3594\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25(180\sqrt{2}+433)}{4(1627+972\sqrt{2})}=1.4316;\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}=\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}\big(1+0.06840\big);

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions \& Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25\sqrt{2}}{8+13\sqrt{2}}=1.34.

(301)

Note the relatively large improvement of 136% for the Coulomb collisions, whereas the improvement for the hard spheres is less than 7%.
Finally, considering the extreme case of the Lorentz approximation yields

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\ll 1;\qquad\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{10825}{868}=12.47;\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}=\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}\big(1+5.485\big)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{433}{204}=2.12;\qquad\quad\,\,\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}=\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}\big(1+0.104\big)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions \& Hard spheres:}\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25}{13}=1.923.

(302)

Here the improvement for the Coulomb collisions is larger than 500%, whereas the improvement for the hard spheres still remains
a marginal 10%.

As noted previously, comparing results in the Lorentz approximation can be slightly confusing, because by using the collisional frequency
\nu_{eb}

and considering full expressions yields

\textrm{Coulomb collisions:}\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\frac{3}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{T_{e}^{5/2}}{\sqrt{m_{e}}e^{4}\ln\Lambda}\,\frac{\gamma_{0}}{Z_{b}};\ \qquad\,\,\,\,\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{2}=\frac{25}{4}\frac{Z_{b}(433Z_{b}+180\sqrt{2})}{(217Z_{b}^{2}+604Z_{b}\sqrt{2}+288)}.

(303)

So as the ion charge
Z_{b}
increases, the parameter
\gamma_{0}
converges to a constant value, but the entire conductivity
\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

actually decreases to zero. Similarly, for the hard spheres
(let us for simplicity use only the 1-Hermite approximation)

\textrm{Hard spheres:}\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\frac{3}{8\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{T_{e}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{m_{e}}}\,\frac{n_{e}\gamma_{0}}{n_{b}(r_{e}+r_{b})^{2}};\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}\big]_{1}=\frac{25}{\frac{32}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{r_{e}^{2}n_{e}}{(r_{e}+r_{b})^{2}n_{b}}+13},

(304)

and as the radius
r_{b}
increases, the parameter
\gamma_{0}
converges to a constant value, but the entire conductivity
\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

decreases to zero.

VIII
Case
m_{a}\gg m_{b}
(heavyweight particles such as ions)

Here we assume
m_{a}\gg m_{b}
and
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}
and consider heavyweight particles “a”, which in addition to self-collisions
also collide with much lighter particles “b”. For the Coulomb collisions,
this section can be viewed as an improved Braginskii ion species, where the ion-electron collisions are retained.
In comparison to the self-collisional Section
VI
, the results will also contain corrections expressed through
the ratio of collisional frequencies

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{Z_{i}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}}};

(305)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\frac{(r_{a}+r_{b})^{2}}{4r_{a}^{2}}\frac{n_{b}}{n_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{b}}{m_{a}}};

(306)

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power}:\qquad\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{K_{ab}}{K_{aa}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\nu-1}}\frac{n_{b}}{n_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{b}}{m_{a}}},

(307)

where for the Coulomb collisions, for additional clarity we changed the indices to ions “i” and electrons “e”. The above ratios might appear
to be small at first sight, where for example for the proton-electron plasma the
\sqrt{2m_{e}/m_{p}}=0.03300
, but as already reported by

[
51
,
52
]
for the Coulomb collisions, the final correction for the Braginskii
parallel ion viscosity is 8% and for the parallel ion thermal conductivity is 18%, which is not insignificant. Here we obtain the improved viscosities and
thermal conductivities through the Chapman-Cowling integrals.

VIII.1
Heavyweight viscosity

Starting with the viscosity, the relevant mass-ratio coefficients (
124
)-(
127
) for
m_{a}\gg m_{b}
simplify into

\displaystyle K_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2;\qquad L_{ab(1)}=\frac{m_{b}}{m_{a}}\Big(1-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Big)\ll 1;\qquad{M}_{ab(1)}=\frac{70}{5};\qquad{N}_{ab(1)}=4,

(308)

and the evolution equations read

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\frac{3}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}+2\nu_{ab}\Big]\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}

(309)

\displaystyle+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big);

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{aa}\big(\frac{21}{10}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}\big)+\frac{70}{5}\nu_{ab}\Big]\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}

(310)

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{aa}(\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24})+4\nu_{ab}\Big]\Big(\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\Big),

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}V_{1}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}+\nu_{aa}V_{2}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a};

(311)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}V_{3}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}+\nu_{aa}V_{4}\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a},

(312)

with the coefficients

\displaystyle V_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{33}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}+2\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big];\qquad V_{2}=\Big[\frac{3}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big];

\displaystyle V_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{77}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{24}-14\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big];\qquad V_{4}=-\Big[\frac{1}{40}\Omega_{22}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}+4\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big].

(313)

For clarity, neglecting the corrections
\nu_{ab}/\nu_{aa}
recovers the self-collisional system (
172
), which in the quasi-static
approximation yielded the self-collisional stress-tensor (
173
)-(
175
).
Here the viscosities of the stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\bm{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
are given by

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\frac{(7V_{2}-V_{4})}{(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})};

(314)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}(V_{1}-7V_{2})+(7V_{2}-V_{4})(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{3}+x(7V_{1}V_{2}-V_{2}V_{3}-7V_{2}V_{4}+V_{4}^{2})\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}(V_{1}^{2}-2V_{2}V_{3}+V_{4}^{2})+(V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4})^{2},

where
x=\Omega_{a}/\nu_{aa}
and re-grouping with the
\nu_{ab}/\nu_{aa}
yields

\displaystyle 7V_{2}-V_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{43}{40}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{23}+\frac{3}{70}\Omega_{24}+4\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}};

\displaystyle V_{2}V_{3}-V_{1}V_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{33}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}+\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(\frac{91}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{3}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big)+8\Big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)^{2};

\displaystyle V_{1}-7V_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\Omega_{22}+2\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}};

\displaystyle 7V_{1}V_{2}-V_{2}V_{3}-7V_{2}V_{4}+V_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2353}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{33}{40}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{81}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(\frac{43}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{12}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{12}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big)+16\Big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)^{2};

\displaystyle V_{1}^{2}-2V_{2}V_{3}+V_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3433}{1600}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{201}{200}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{129}{1400}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{99}{700}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{9}{350}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{9}{4900}\Omega_{24}^{2}

(315)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(11\Omega_{22}-\frac{12}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{12}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big)+20\Big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)^{2}.

The parallel viscosity can be slightly re-arranged into

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{6}\frac{(301\Omega_{22}-84\Omega_{23}+12\Omega_{24}+1120\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})}{[77\Omega_{22}^{2}+6\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-6\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}(\frac{3185}{3}\Omega_{22}-140\Omega_{23}+20\Omega_{24})+\frac{5600}{3}(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})^{2}]}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}.

(316)

The results (
314
)-(
316
) now represent the stress-tensor for heavyweight particles, where collisions with much lighter particles are retained.
Prescribing the Coulomb collisions (with
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\nu_{ii}}\,\frac{\frac{41}{28}+4\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}}{\frac{267}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+8(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}})^{2}};

(317)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}\Big(\frac{6}{5}+2\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{41}{28}+4\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\Big)\Big(\frac{267}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+8\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}x}{\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}+\frac{46561}{19600}+\frac{82}{7}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+16\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{79321}{19600}+\frac{578}{35}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+20\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big]+\Big[\frac{267}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+8\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big]^{2},

where the ratio of collisional frequencies is given by (
305
). We will analyze the result further below, but first we find it entertaining to write down
the solutions for the generalized hard spheres (where the parallel viscosity is fully meaningfull), which reads

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{\frac{41}{28}+4\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}}{\frac{303}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+8(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})^{2}};

(318)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}\Big(\frac{6}{5}+2\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{41}{28}+4\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)\Big(\frac{303}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+8\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}x}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}+\frac{42529}{19600}+\frac{82}{7}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+16\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{71257}{19600}+\frac{578}{35}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+20\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big]+\Big[\frac{303}{175}+\frac{541}{70}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+8\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big]^{2},

and the ratio of collisional frequencies is given by (
306
). Note the many numerical similarities between the Coulomb collisions (
317
)
and the hard spheres (
318
) and the parallel viscosities can be also written as

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1025+2800\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}}{1068+5410\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+5600(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}})^{2}}\,\frac{p_{i}}{\nu_{ii}};

(319)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1025+2800\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}}{1212+5410\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+5600(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})^{2}}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}.

(320)

From the expressions (
319
)-(
320
) it is obvious that even though the ratios
\nu_{ie}/\nu_{ii}
might be small, they are multiplied by quite large
numbers. Note that because the ratio
\nu_{ie}/\nu_{ii}
depends on the ion mass
m_{i}
, one can not just write numerical results for the ion charge
Z_{i}=1

(as it was possible for the electron species, where the ratio
\nu_{ei}/\nu_{ee}=Z_{i}\sqrt{2}
does not contain the ion mass) and to get some numerical
values, one needs to choose a particular case. Considering the proton-electron plasma yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions,}\,\,\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}=0.033;\qquad\eta_{0}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.892\,\frac{p_{i}}{\nu_{ii}};\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+4.614x^{2}+3.202;

(321)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,\Big[1.266x^{2}+2.8565\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}x}{\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}+2.780\Big].

Contrasting the result (
321
) with the self-collisional eq. (4.44) of
[
11
]
or the eq. (74) of
[
43
]
reveals that
the numerical values are quite different, implying that the effects of the ion-electron collisions are not completely insignificant.
For example, the change of the parallel viscosity value from the 1025/1068=0.960 into the 0.892 represents a difference of almost 8%
(when divided by the smaller value).
The value of 0.892 is consistent with the eq. (217) of Part 1, there calculated even more precisely without any expansions in small mass-ratios.
As another double-check, it can be shown that our Coulomb viscosities yield the same results as equations (89a)-(89c) of
[
51
]

(where we only consider
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}
), which is shown below in Section
VIII.3
.

For completeness, considering the hard spheres with the same ratio of collisional frequencies as in (
321
), yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres'',}\,\,\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}=0.033;\qquad\eta_{0}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.800\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+4.202x^{2}+3.981;

(322)

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[1.266x^{2}+3.1849\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}x}{\nu_{aa}\Delta}\,\Big[x^{2}+2.574\Big],

and for the parallel viscosity the change from the self-collisional value of 1025/1212=0.846 into the 0.800 represents a difference of 6%.

VIII.2
Heavyweight thermal conductivity

Continuing with the thermal conductivity, the relevant mass ratio coefficients (
128
)-(
131
) for
m_{a}\gg m_{b}
simplify into

\displaystyle D_{ab(1)}=3;\qquad E_{ab(1)}\ll 1;\qquad F_{ab(1)}=84;\qquad G_{ab(1)}=5,

(323)

and the evolution equations become

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\frac{2}{5}\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}+3\nu_{ab}\Big]\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

(324)

\displaystyle+\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big)\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big);

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{28}{5}\Omega_{22}\Big)+84\nu_{ab}\Big]\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

(325)

\displaystyle-\Big[\nu_{aa}\Big(\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}\Big)+5\nu_{ab}\Big]\Big(\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big),

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}B_{1}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}+\nu_{aa}B_{2}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a};

(326)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}B_{3}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}-\nu_{aa}B_{4}\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a},

(327)

with the coefficients

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{9}{5}\Omega_{22}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}+3\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big];\qquad B_{2}=\Big[\frac{1}{20}\Omega_{22}-\frac{1}{70}\Omega_{23}\Big];

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}+14\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{24}-56\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big];\qquad B_{4}=\Big[\frac{2}{35}\Omega_{24}-\frac{3}{10}\Omega_{22}+5\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big].

(328)

The quasi-static approximation then yields the thermal heat flux

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a}\bm{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a};

(329)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\gamma_{0};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{a}=\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle};\qquad\triangle=x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0},

with the transport coefficients (which should not be confused with the electron species)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5(28B_{2}+B_{4})}{2(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{5}{2}B_{1}-70B_{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{5}{2}(28B_{2}+B_{4})(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3});

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{B_{2}}{2}(140B_{1}-5B_{3}+140B_{4})+\frac{5}{2}B_{4}^{2};

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{1}^{2}-2B_{2}B_{3}+B_{4}^{2};\qquad\delta_{0}=(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})^{2},

(330)

and the thermal conductivities become

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\Delta^{*}}\Big(\frac{11}{4}\Omega_{22}-\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big);

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})=\frac{4}{175}\Big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{24}\Omega_{22}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(\frac{53}{10}\Omega_{22}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{23}+\frac{6}{35}\Omega_{24}\Big)+15\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big\{x^{2}\Big(\Omega_{22}+\frac{15}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{11}{4}\Omega_{22}-\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)\Delta^{*}\Big\};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}x}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big\{\frac{5}{2}x^{2}+\frac{149}{40}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{13}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{11}{35}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{22}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{23}+\frac{2}{245}\Omega_{24}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(\frac{55}{2}\Omega_{22}-10\Omega_{23}+\frac{10}{7}\Omega_{24}\Big)+\frac{125}{2}\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{193}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{22}{175}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{22}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{24}\Omega_{23}+\frac{4}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}

(331)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big(\frac{67}{5}\Omega_{22}-4\Omega_{23}+\frac{4}{7}\Omega_{24}\Big)+34\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big]+(\Delta^{*})^{2}.

The parallel thermal conductivity can be also written as

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25}{2}\frac{(77\Omega_{22}-28\Omega_{23}+4\Omega_{24}+350\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})}{\big[56\Omega_{22}^{2}+8\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-8\Omega_{23}^{2}+\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big(1855\Omega_{22}-420\Omega_{23}+60\Omega_{24}\big)+5250\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\big]}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}.

(332)

Prescribing the Coulomb collisions (with
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}}\frac{\frac{45}{14}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}}{\frac{144}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+15(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}})^{2}};

(333)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}\Big\{x^{2}\Big(2+\frac{15}{2}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{45}{14}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\Big)\Big[\frac{144}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+15\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}x}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}\Big\{\frac{5}{2}x^{2}+\frac{2277}{490}+\frac{225}{7}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+\frac{125}{2}\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{3313}{1225}+\frac{618}{35}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+34\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}\Big]+\Big[\frac{144}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+15(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}})^{2}\Big]^{2},

with the ratio of collisional frequencies (
305
). For comparison, prescribing the generalized hard spheres (the parallel thermal
conductivity is fully meaningfull) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\frac{\frac{45}{14}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}}{\frac{176}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+15(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})^{2}};

(334)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big\{x^{2}\Big(2+\frac{15}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{45}{14}+\frac{25}{2}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\Big)\Big[\frac{176}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+15\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}x}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big\{\frac{5}{2}x^{2}+\frac{2053}{490}+\frac{225}{7}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+\frac{125}{2}\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{573}{245}+\frac{618}{35}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+34\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}\Big]+\Big[\frac{176}{175}+\frac{55}{7}\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+15(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}})^{2}\Big]^{2},

with the ratio of collisional frequencies (
306
). Note the many numerical similarities between the (
333
)-(
334
) and
the parallel thermal conductivities can be also written as

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1125+4375\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}}{288+2750\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}+5250\big(\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}\big)^{2}}\,\frac{p_{i}}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}};

(335)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1125+4375\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}}{352+2750\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}+5250\big(\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}\big)^{2}}\,\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}.

(336)

Considering the proton-electron plasma with the ratio of collisional frequencies
\nu_{ie}/\nu_{ii}=0.033
, the Coulomb collisions then yield
in the magnetized case

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions,}\,\,\frac{\nu_{ie}}{\nu_{ii}}=0.033;\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3.302\frac{p_{i}}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}};\qquad\triangle=x^{4}+3.3242x^{2}+1.2067;

(337)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}\Big[2.2475x^{2}+3.9839\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}x}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{2}+5.7757\Big].

Contrasting these conductivities with the self-collisional eq. (4.40) of
[
11
]
or eq. (44) in
[
43
]
reveals that some values
are very different. For example, for the parallel conductivity the change from the self-collisional value of 1125/288 = 125/32 = 3.906 into the above value
3.302 represents a difference of 18% (when divided by the smaller value).
The value 3.302 is consistent with the eq. (214) in Part 1, there calculated without any expansions in mass-ratios.
From the numerical values (
337
), the largest correction is in the value
\delta_{0}=1.2067
,
where the self-collisional Braginskii result was
\delta_{0}=0.6771
.
For example, considering the cross conductivity in the limit of weak magnetic field
(
x\ll 1
), where the
\kappa_{\times}^{i}
is small, yields

\textrm{Weak B-field}:\quad\kappa_{\times}^{i}=\frac{p_{i}x}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}}\underbrace{\frac{5.7757}{1.2067}}_{=4.787};\qquad\big(\kappa_{\times}^{i}\big)^{\textrm{Self-coll}}=\frac{p_{i}x}{m_{i}\nu_{ii}}\underbrace{\frac{4.6469}{0.6771}}_{=6.863},

(338)

which is a difference of 43%. It is indeed counter-intuitive that collisions with particles that are 1836 times lighter can introduce such large differences.
For the Coulomb collisions, equivalence with
[
51
]
(where we only consider
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}
) is shown below in Section
VIII.3
.

For completeness, considering the hard spheres with the same ratio of collisional frequencies as in (
337
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres'',}\,\,\frac{\nu_{ab}}{\nu_{aa}}=0.033;\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2.830\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}};\qquad\triangle=x^{4}+2.958x^{2}+1.642;

(339)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big[2.2475x^{2}+4.647\Big];

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}x}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}\triangle}\Big[\frac{5}{2}x^{2}+5.3186\Big].

The result can be contrasted with the self-collisional eq. (
201
), where the parallel conductivity of hard spheres was 3.196, which
represents difference of 13%. For the
\kappa_{\times}^{a}
in the limit of weak magnetic field, the difference is 28%.

VIII.3
Comparison with Ji and Held 2013 (Coulomb collisions)

As a double-check that our model is formulated correctly, it is useful to compare our results with
[
51
]
.
As discussed already in the Introduction, even though our general model formulated through the evolution equations is valid for arbitrary temperature differences,
we prefer to write down quasi-static solutions only for the similar temperatures
T_{a}\simeq T_{b}
, so that the expansions with mass-ratios remain valid.
For large temperature differences, especially if the ion temperature vastly exceeds the electron temperature, it is better to obtain the
quasi-static approximation numerically, without any expansions in mass-ratios.
That the case of large temperature differences is indeed not trivial, can be also seen from the discussion in
[
52
]
.
To compare our results with the former reference, we thus introduce the variable
\zeta=(1/Z_{i})\sqrt{m_{e}/m_{i}}
, so that our
\nu_{ii}/\nu_{ie}=\sqrt{2}\zeta
.
Their choice of the ion collisional time differs from our/Braginskii choice
by
(1/\tau_{ii})^{\textrm{JH}}=\sqrt{2}/\tau_{ii}=\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}
(for a further discussion about this topic, see Section 8.2, p. 31 in
[
43
]
).
So by using their variable
r=x/\sqrt{2}
and by keeping our collisional frequencies, one can write the ion viscosities (
319
) and (
317
) as

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}\frac{1025}{1068}+\frac{1400}{267}\zeta}{1+\frac{2705}{534}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{2800}{267}\zeta^{2}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,4\Big[r^{2}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\sqrt{2}+2\zeta\Big)+\Big(\sqrt{2}\frac{1025}{1068}+\frac{1400}{267}\zeta\Big)\frac{1}{4}(\frac{267}{175})^{2}\Big(1+\frac{2705}{534}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{2800}{267}\zeta^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}r}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,4\Big[r^{2}+\frac{46561}{39200}+\frac{41}{7}\sqrt{2}\zeta+16\zeta^{2}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big\{r^{4}+r^{2}\Big[\frac{79321}{39200}+\frac{289}{35}\sqrt{2}\zeta+20\zeta^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{4}(\frac{267}{175})^{2}\Big[1+\frac{2705}{534}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{2800}{267}\zeta^{2}\Big]^{2}\Big\},

and numerical evaluation yields

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}}\,\frac{1.357+5.243\zeta}{1+7.164\zeta+10.487\zeta^{2}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,4\Big[r^{2}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\sqrt{2}+2\zeta\Big)+\Big(1.357+5.243\zeta\Big)0.582\Big(1+7.164\zeta+10.487\zeta^{2}\Big)\Big];

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}r}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ii}\Delta}\,4\Big[r^{2}+1.188+8.283\zeta+16\zeta^{2}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big\{r^{4}+r^{2}\Big[2.023+11.677\zeta+20\zeta^{2}\Big]+0.582\Big[1+7.164\zeta+10.487\zeta^{2}\Big]^{2}\Big\},

recovering equations (89a)-(89c) of
[
51
]
(there is a small missprint in their
e\to i
). Similarly, one can write
the ion thermal conductivities (
335
) and (
333
) as

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}}\,\frac{\frac{125}{32}\sqrt{2}+\frac{4375}{144}\zeta}{1+\frac{1375}{144}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{875}{24}\zeta^{2}};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}4\Big\{r^{2}\Big(\sqrt{2}+\frac{15}{2}\zeta\Big)+\Big(\frac{125}{32}\sqrt{2}+\frac{4375}{144}\zeta\Big)\frac{1}{4}\big(\frac{144}{175}\big)^{2}\Big[1+\frac{1375}{144}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{875}{24}\zeta^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}r}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}4\Big\{\frac{5}{2}r^{2}+\frac{2277}{980}+\frac{225}{14}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{125}{2}\zeta^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big\{r^{4}+r^{2}\Big[\frac{3313}{2450}+\frac{309}{35}\sqrt{2}\zeta+34\zeta^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{4}\big(\frac{144}{175}\big)^{2}\Big[1+\frac{1375}{144}\sqrt{2}\zeta+\frac{875}{24}\zeta^{2}\Big]^{2}\Big\},

and numerical evaluation yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}}\,\frac{5.524+30.382\zeta}{1+13.504\zeta+36.458\zeta^{2}};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\perp}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}4\Big\{r^{2}\Big(\sqrt{2}+\frac{15}{2}\zeta\Big)+\Big(5.524+30.382\zeta\Big)0.1693\Big[1+13.504\zeta+36.458\zeta^{2}\Big]\Big\};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\times}^{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{i}r}{\sqrt{2}m_{i}\nu_{ii}\triangle}4\Big\{\frac{5}{2}r^{2}+2.323+22.728\zeta+62.500\zeta^{2}\Big\};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big\{r^{4}+r^{2}\Big[1.352+12.485\zeta+34\zeta^{2}\Big]+0.1693\Big[1+13.504\zeta+36.458\zeta^{2}\Big]^{2}\Big\},

recovering equations (88a)-(88c) of
[
51
]
. They also provide solutions in the 3-Hermite approximation.

IX
Scalar perturbation (excess-kurtosis)
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

Here we consider solutions for the scalar perturbations
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
, which we separate into two cases of self-collisions
and of lightweight particles
m_{a}\ll m_{b}
(and we do not discuss corrections for the heavyweight particles
m_{a}\gg m_{b}
).

IX.1
Scalar
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
for self-collisions

At the semi-linear level, the evolution equation for the scalar perturbation reads (see eq. (
81
) with collisional contributions (
118
))

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a},

(340)

which in the quasi-static approximation yields solution

\displaystyle\textrm{22-mom:}\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5}{2\nu_{aa}\Omega_{22}}\Big(\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\Big),

(341)

where the (2-Hermite) heat fluxes
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
are given by (
198
) and (
209
).
As noted below eq. (
81
), we neglected the contributions of
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
in the heat flux evolution equations, resulting
in suppression of terms such as
\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\sim\nabla\nabla^{2}T_{a}
(in the unmagnetized case). Considering the magnetized case, at the semi-linear level one can simplify

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}=-\,\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{a}+\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{a}\big);\qquad\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}=-\,\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{a}+\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{a}\big),

(342)

and the general solution for the
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
then has a form

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\rho_{a}}\Big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{a}+\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{a}\Big);

(343)

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\parallel}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{125(63\Omega_{22}-21\Omega_{23}+2\Omega_{24})}{2\Omega_{22}(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2})}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\perp}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\frac{5(2\Omega_{23}-3\Omega_{22})}{\Omega_{22}}x^{2}+\frac{8(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2})(63\Omega_{22}-21\Omega_{23}+2\Omega_{24})}{245\Omega_{22}}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+x^{2}\Big[\frac{193}{100}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{6}{5}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{23}+\frac{22}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{36}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{24}+\frac{4}{1225}\Omega_{24}^{2}\Big]

(344)

\displaystyle+\Big[\frac{4}{175}\big(7\Omega_{22}^{2}+\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}-\Omega_{23}^{2}\big)\Big]^{2},

where the Hall parameter
x=\Omega_{a}/\nu_{aa}
. The
\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}
and
\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}
can be viewed as the thermal conductivities
of the 4th-order fluid moment and can be also written as

\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}-20\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big);\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\big(\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}-20\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\big).

(345)

Evaluating (
344
) for the case of the Coulomb collisions (with
\ln\Lambda\gg 1
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad{\kappa}_{\parallel}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1375}{24}}_{57.29}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\perp}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[5x^{2}+\frac{9504}{245}\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{3313}{1225}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{144}{175}\Big)^{2},

(346)

recovering equations (149)-(150) of
[
43
]
. For comparison, evaluating (
344
) for the case of the
generalized hard spheres yields (the parallel conductivity is fully meaningful)

\displaystyle\textrm{``Hard spheres''}:\qquad{\kappa}_{\parallel}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{2375}{88}}_{26.99}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\perp}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[25x^{2}+\frac{6688}{245}\Big];\qquad\Delta=x^{4}+\frac{573}{245}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{176}{175}\Big)^{2}.

(347)

Finally, evaluating (
344
) for the inverse power-law force yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Inverse power}:\qquad{\kappa}_{\parallel}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{125(19\nu^{2}-32\nu+21)(\nu-1)}{2(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)^{2}}\Big(\frac{A_{1}(\nu)}{A_{2}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\perp}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}\Delta}\Big[\frac{5(5\nu-9)}{(\nu-1)}x^{2}+\frac{8(3\nu-5)^{2}(11\nu-13)(19\nu^{2}-32\nu+21)}{245(\nu-1)^{5}}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\Big];

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\frac{(3\nu-5)^{2}(2865\nu^{4}-13348\nu^{3}+25446\nu^{2}-22820\nu+8113)}{4900(\nu-1)^{6}}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}x^{2}

(348)

\displaystyle+\Big[\frac{4(11\nu-13)(3\nu-5)^{2}}{175(\nu-1)^{3}}\Big(\frac{A_{2}(\nu)}{A_{1}(\nu)}\Big)^{2}\Big]^{2}.

As discussed already in the Introduction, the above 22-moment model represents a 2-Hermite and 1-Hermite hybrid model, because the heat fluxes are described by
two Hermite polynomials (and are analogous to the Braginskii precision), whereas the scalar perturbations are described by one Hermite polynomial (see also the
limitations Section
X.6.2
, where the 23-moment model is briefly discussed).

IX.1.1
Reduction into 14-moment model

It is useful to briefly explore the influence of the 2-Hermite heat fluxes, by reducing the 22-moment model into the 14-moment model, where the heat fluxes
are also described by only 1-Hermite polynomial. By prescribing closure
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{a}=28(p_{a}/\rho_{a})\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}
, the evolution equation (
340
) in the
1-Hermite approximation reads

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+8\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{aa}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a},

(349)

where the 1-Hermite heat flux
\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\big]_{1}
is given by (
204
).
The quasi-static approximation then yields the solution

\displaystyle\textrm{14-mom:}\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{20}{\Omega_{22}}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{a}\big]_{1},

(350)

which at the semi-linear level simplifies into the same form as (
343
)

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=+\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\rho_{a}}\Big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{a}+\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{a}\Big),

(351)

but now the 1-Hermite thermal conductivities (of the 4th-order fluid moment) read

\displaystyle\big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{125}{\Omega_{22}^{2}}\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};\qquad\big[\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}\big]_{1}=\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}\,\frac{20}{x^{2}+\big(2\Omega_{22}/5\big)^{2}}.

(352)

Note that for both the Coulomb collisions and the hard spheres the (
352
) has the same form (because
\Omega_{22}=2
)
and for example the parallel value reads
125/4=31.250
, which is quite different from the Coulomb value of
57.29
given by (
346
),
representing a correction of 83%. In contrast, the hard sphere value of
26.99
given by (
347
) is much closer to the 1-Hermite result
(with a negative correction of 14%).

Thus, already from the self-collisional case it is possible to conclude that the scalar perturbations
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
are far more sensitive to the
choice of the Hermite approximation than the heat fluxes or stress-tensors and it seems that it is necessary to consider the 23-moment model
(see Section
X.6.2
) or possibly beyond to obtain a more reliable
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
values (the convergence was studied by
[
48
]
, but
we were unable to deduce the converged
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
value from their work).

IX.2
Scalar
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}
for lightweight particles
m_{e}\ll m_{b}

Considering the case
m_{e}\ll m_{b}
with temperatures
T_{e}\simeq T_{b}
, the mass-ratio coefficients
S_{ab}
(
133
)
entering the collisional exchange rates
\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
(
118
)
are small in comparison to the self-collisions (of the order of
m_{e}/m_{b}
or smaller).
The evolution equation for the scalar
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}
thus has the same form as (
340
)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-20\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{ee}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e},

(353)

with the quasi-static solution

\displaystyle\textrm{22-mom:}\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5}{2\nu_{ee}\Omega_{22}}\Big(\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-20\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\Big),

(354)

but now the (2-Hermite) heat fluxes
\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}
for the lightweight particles are given by
(
254
)-(
261
) with the B-coefficients (
253
), which contain the Chapman-Cowling integrals.
For the particular case of Coulomb collisions, the solution (
354
) recovers eq. (154) of
[
43
]
. We here directly simplify
the (
354
) by further applying the semi-linear approximation,
with the thermal and frictional parts (
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}=\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}
and
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}=\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)T}_{e}+\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)u}_{e}
and

\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}=\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}+\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}
)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\big);

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\Big[\beta_{0}^{(5)}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u})\Big];

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\big);

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{0}p_{e}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+p_{e}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-p_{e}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u});

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0},

(355)

where the
\delta\bm{u}=\bm{u}_{e}-\bm{u}_{b}
, the
x=\Omega_{e}/\nu_{eb}
and all the other coefficients given by (
254
)-(
261
).
The thermal part of (
354
) then becomes (changing from
\nu_{ee}
to
\nu_{eb}
to make easy comparison with Part 1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{eb}\rho_{e}}\Big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\Big);

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}(\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}-20\kappa_{\parallel}^{e});\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}(\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}-20\kappa_{\perp}^{e}),

(356)

where the thermal conductivities (of the 4th-order fluid moment) are analogous to eq. (157) of Part 1.
It is useful to introduce the
\gamma
-coefficients (of the 4th-order fluid moment) and write the thermal conductivities as

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\gamma_{0}^{(4)};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle};

(357)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\gamma_{0}^{(5)}-20\gamma_{0}\big);\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\gamma_{1}^{\prime}\big);\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\gamma_{0}^{\prime}\big),

(358)

which are analogous to equations (159) and (162) of Part 1. These
\gamma
-coefficients then can be expressed through the Chapman-Cowling integrals explicitly, where the
parallel coefficient reads

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}\triangle^{*}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(36\Omega_{22}-12\Omega_{23}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{24}\Big)+175-245\Omega_{12}+114\Omega_{13}-20\Omega_{14}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{15}\Big];

(359)

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(B_{1}B_{4}+B_{2}B_{3})=\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}\Big(\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{24}+\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{22}^{2}-\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{23}^{2}\Big)+\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}-\frac{4}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{24}

(360)

\displaystyle+\frac{11}{25}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-\frac{4}{25}\Omega_{14}\Omega_{22}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{22}\Omega_{15}-\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}-\frac{8}{175}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{14}+\frac{48}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{24}+\Omega_{22}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}-\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}+\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+\frac{4}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}+\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{15}-\frac{4}{175}\Omega_{14}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{19}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}+\frac{1}{7}\Omega_{15}+\frac{7}{4}\Omega_{13}-\Omega_{14},

and the perpendicular coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\big(4\Omega_{23}-6\Omega_{22}\big)-\frac{75}{2}+65\Omega_{12}-30\Omega_{13}+4\Omega_{14}\Big];

(361)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\triangle^{*}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(36\Omega_{22}-12\Omega_{23}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{24}\Big)+175-245\Omega_{12}+114\Omega_{13}-20\Omega_{14}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{15}\Big]=\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\triangle^{*2}.

(362)

As a summary, the thermal part reads

\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}=+\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e}}\Big(\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\Big);\qquad\triangle=x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0},

(363)

with the
\gamma
-coefficients given by (
359
)-(
362
) and the
\delta
-coefficients
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{0}
given by (
273
),
which fully expresses the thermal part through the Chapman-Cowling integrals.
As a double-check, prescribing Coulomb collisions recovers the
\gamma
-coefficients (164) of Part 1. Also, in the limit of zero magnetic field
the result (
363
) simplifies into an isotropic
\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}=(p_{e}^{2}/\nu_{eb}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e})\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\nabla^{2}T_{e}
.
For comparison, the parallel coefficients can be also written as

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{250(132\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+229)}{(576\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}+1208\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+217)};

(364)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{1000(19\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+17)}{(704\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}+1944\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1275)},

(365)

and to have some numerical values, for the particular case of

\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\quad\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\gamma_{0}^{(4)}=83.847;\qquad\textrm{Hard spheres}:\gamma_{0}^{(4)}=14.339.

(366)

A similar construction can be done for the frictional part
\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}
, by using the
\beta
-coefficients (
257
) and (
261
)
and defining

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}\rho_{e}}\Big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u})\Big];

(367)

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)}-20\beta_{0}\big);\qquad\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\beta_{1}^{\prime}\big);\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\beta_{0}^{\prime}\big);

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big);\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\big),

(368)

which is analogous to the equations (158) and (168) of Part 1. All of the
\beta
-coefficients are then expressed through the Chapman-Cowling
integrals easily, where for example the parallel coefficient reads

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2\Omega_{22}\Delta^{*}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\Big(8\Omega_{22}-\frac{16}{5}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{22}-8\Omega_{23}+\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{23}+\frac{8}{5}\Omega_{23}\Omega_{12}+8\Omega_{12}\Omega_{22}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{24}-\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{24}\Big)

(369)

\displaystyle-42\Omega_{12}^{2}+\frac{24}{5}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{14}-\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{12}\Omega_{15}+16\Omega_{13}\Omega_{12}+\frac{16}{35}\Omega_{13}\Omega_{14}-\frac{208}{35}\Omega_{13}^{2}+\frac{8}{7}\Omega_{15}+42\Omega_{13}-16\Omega_{14}\Big],

with the
\Delta^{*}
given by (
360
). Evaluating the (
369
) then yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{150(32\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+29)}{(576\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}+1208\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+217)};

(370)

\displaystyle\textrm{Hard spheres}:\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{50(64\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+51)}{(704\frac{\nu_{ee}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}}+1944\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+1275)},

(371)

and to have some numerical values, for the particular case of

\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\quad\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\beta_{0}^{(4)}=8.06;\qquad\textrm{Hard spheres}:\beta_{0}^{(4)}=-2.27.

(372)

In the limit of zero magnetic field, the solution (
367
) simplifies into an isotropic

\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}=-(p_{e}^{2}/\nu_{eb}\rho_{e})\beta_{0}^{(4)}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}
.

IX.2.1
Reduction into 14-moment model

Prescribing closure
\vec{\bm{X}}^{(5)}_{e}=28(p_{e}/\rho_{e})\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}
in equations (
353
)-(
354
)
yields a quasi-static solution

\displaystyle\textrm{14-mom:}\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ee}\rho_{e}}\frac{20}{\Omega_{22}}\nabla\cdot\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\big]_{1},

(373)

where the 1-Hermite heat flux
\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}\big]_{1}
is given by (
294
)-(
295
).
Further applying the semi-linear approximation yields the thermal and frictional parts

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{T}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla^{2}_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla^{2}_{\perp}T_{e};\qquad\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}=\frac{5}{2B_{1}}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e}=\frac{5}{2}\frac{B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{eb}};

(374)

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\big[\vec{\bm{q}}_{e}^{u}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{5}}{B_{1}}p_{e}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+p_{e}\frac{B_{5}B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-p_{e}\frac{B_{5}x}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\nabla\cdot(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u});

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{22}+\frac{5}{2}-2\Omega_{12}+\frac{2}{5}\Omega_{13}\Big];\qquad B_{5}=\big(\frac{5}{2}-\Omega_{12}\big),

(375)

and the thermal part of (
373
) then can be written as (changing to
\nu_{eb}
similarly to (
363
))

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e}}\Big(\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\frac{50}{\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\Big);\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)}=\frac{50}{\Omega_{22}B_{1}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}},

(376)

and the frictional part of (
373
) reads (similarly to (
367
))

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{eb}\rho_{e}}\Big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{20}{\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{B_{5}B_{1}}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\nabla\cdot\delta\bm{u}_{\perp}-\frac{20}{\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{B_{5}x}{(x^{2}+B_{1}^{2})}\nabla\cdot(\bm{\hat{b}}\times\delta\bm{u})\Big];

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{20}{\Omega_{22}}\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{B_{5}}{B_{1}},

(377)

with the
B_{1}
and
B_{5}
given by (
375
). The evaluation of the parallel coefficients yields (emphasizing the 1-Hermite approximation with brackets
[\ldots]_{1}
)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions and Hard spheres}:\qquad\big[\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{250}{(8\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+13)};

(378)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\quad\big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{150}{(8\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+13)};\qquad\textrm{Hard spheres}:\quad\big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=-\,\frac{\nu_{eb}}{\nu_{ee}}\frac{50}{(8\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}+13)},

(379)

and to have some numerical values, for the particular case of

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{ee}}{\nu_{eb}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions and Hard spheres}:\big[\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=18.95;

(381)

\displaystyle\textrm{Coulomb collisions}:\big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=11.37;\qquad\textrm{Hard spheres}:\big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\big]_{1}=-3.79.

Comparing the numerical values with the previously obtained (
366
) and (
372
) reveals that for the Coulomb collisions,
the 2-Hermite heat fluxes in the 22-moment model have a huge influence at the thermal coefficient
\gamma_{0}^{(4)}
,
where the 14-moment value of
18.95
increases over 4.4 times into the value of
83.847
. It seems that the 23-moment model yields a further drastic increase,
see the limitations Section
X.6.2
and eq. (
414
).

X
Discussion and Conclusions

Here we discuss various topics that we find of interest.

X.1
Numerical constants
A_{l}(\nu)
for repulsive forces

Detailed discussions on how to solve the scattering process for a central force can be found in various books,
which we are not interested in repeating here, see for example
[
18
]
, p. 170, or
[
111
]
, p. 77
(we use the notation of the last reference where the scattering angle is
\theta
and not
\chi
).
The numerical values
A_{1}(\nu)
and
A_{2}(\nu)
for repulsive forces which enter the Chapman-Cowling integrals
are given in the first reference in Table 3, p. 172 and in the second reference on p. 103 and
also in
[
42
]
, p. 548.
However, a small complication arises because our multi-fluid 21- and 22-moment models also require the
A_{3}(\nu)
numbers, which are not given by the
above references and we have to provide these numbers, to make our model usable.
(Note that the Chapman-Cowling table contains a 3rd column with numbers
A
, but this number is unrelated to
A_{3}(\nu)
and defined as (
47
)).
Also, the above tables can be quite confusing for a newcomer,
because the tables only consider
\nu\geq 5
and at the first sight it is not clear, if all of the forces below
\nu<5
require the Coulomb logarithm cut-off,
or if the “trouble” starts only exactly at the case
\nu=2
(the latter is true, and no cut-off is required for
\nu=2.1
).
We thus created a new Table
2
, which contains the required
A_{3}(\nu)
numbers and for clarity, we also included the
\nu=3
and
\nu=4
cases.
Later on, we found the cases
\nu=3
and
\nu=4
, calculated also for the
A_{3}(\nu)
in Table 1 of
[
67
]

and more precise results can be found in Table 2 of
[
41
]
.

Let us summarize a quick recipe how the numbers
A_{l}(\nu)
are calculated.
For any interaction potential
V(r)
, the distance of the closest approach is calculated by solving the equation

1-\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{r^{2}}-\frac{2V(r)}{\mu_{ab}g_{ab}^{2}}=0,

(382)

where
b_{0}
is the impact parameter,
\mu_{ab}
the reduced mass and
\bm{g}_{ab}=\bm{v}_{a}-\bm{v}_{b}
the relative velocity.
The repulsive force
F_{ab}=K_{ab}/r^{\nu}
(where
K_{ab}
is positive) corresponds
to potential
V(r)=\frac{K_{ab}}{(\nu-1)r^{\nu-1}}
and it
is useful to introduce normalization parameter
\alpha_{0}=(\frac{K_{ab}}{\mu_{ab}g_{ab}^{2}})^{1/(\nu-1)}
. Then, by
adopting the notation of Chapman-Cowling with the dimensionless quantity
v=b_{0}/r
and the normalized impact parameter
v_{0}=b_{0}/\alpha_{0}

(which is equivalent to our
\hat{b}_{0}
), the recipe consists of finding the
v
value which satisfies the equation

1-v^{2}-\frac{2}{\nu-1}\Big(\frac{v}{v_{0}}\Big)^{\nu-1}=0,

(383)

and denoting the real positive solution as
v_{00}
. Then, the relation between the scattering angle
\theta
and the
normalized impact parameter
v_{0}
is calculated according to

\theta=\pi-2\Phi;\qquad\Phi=\int_{0}^{v_{00}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-v^{2}-\frac{2}{\nu-1}(v/v_{0})^{\nu-1}}}dv.

(384)

For example, for the Coulomb collisions (
\nu=2
) one obtains
v_{00}=(-1+\sqrt{v_{0}^{2}+1})/v_{0}
, leading to the
relation
\theta=2\arcsin(1/\sqrt{v_{0}^{2}+1})
, which can be rewritten as
\cos\theta=(v_{0}^{2}+1)/(v_{0}^{2}-1)
or equivalently
as
\tan(\theta/2)=1/v_{0}=\alpha_{0}/b_{0}=q_{a}q_{b}/(\mu_{ab}g^{2}b_{0})
.

2
2

2

For attractive forces, the recipe is modified by changing the signs in front of
2/(\nu-1)
in (
383
)-(
384
) to plus signs and replacing

K_{ab}
with
|K_{ab}|
.
For the Coulomb collisions one obtains
v_{00}=(1+\sqrt{v_{0}^{2}+1})/v_{0}
, leading to the
relation
\theta=-2\arcsin(1/\sqrt{v_{0}^{2}+1})
, so that the scattering angle
\theta
is negative, however the
\cos\theta=(v_{0}^{2}+1)/(v_{0}^{2}-1)
is the same, because

\cos(-x)=\cos(x)
. One can also write
\tan(\theta/2)=-1/v_{0}=-|q_{a}q_{b}|/(\mu_{ab}g^{2}b_{0})=+q_{a}q_{b}/(\mu_{ab}g^{2}b_{0})
, which is again
the same result as for the repulsive case.

The repulsive case
\nu=3
is addressed in the next section. Unfortunately for a general
\nu
, the relation (
384
) can not be
obtained in primitive functions and the relationship between the scattering angle
\theta
and the normalized impact parameter
v_{0}
is only
numerical, i.e. one prescribes some concrete
v_{0}
and numerically obtains the corresponding
\theta
.
Note that without solving the relation (
384
), one can not write the differential cross-section
\sigma_{ab}(g,\theta)
either.
Nevertheless, the effective cross-sections
\mathbb{Q}_{ab}^{(l)}
integrate over all the possible normalized
impact parameters
v_{0}
, and it is possible to put all of the numerical factors inside of the constants

A_{l}(\nu)=\int_{0}^{\infty}(1-\cos^{l}\theta)v_{0}dv_{0},

(385)

which are pure numbers. Here, we have already used for the upper boundary the usual
\infty
, because the cases
of hard spheres (with the cut-off
v_{0}^{\textrm{max}}=1
) and Coulomb collisions (with the cut-off
v_{0}^{\textrm{max}}=\Lambda
)
were already addressed, and all the other cases (for
\nu>2
) do not require a cut-off. By plugging the (
384
) into (
385
),
for a given
\nu
and
l
, we need to numerically integrate

A_{l}(\nu)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{1-(-1)^{l}\cos^{l}\Big[\int_{0}^{v_{00}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{1-v^{2}-\frac{2}{\nu-1}(v/v_{0})^{\nu-1}}}dv\Big]\Big\}v_{0}dv_{0}.

(386)

To solve the (
386
), for the inner integral we have used the built-in numerical integration in the Maple software. For the outer integral,
we have written a very primitive “midpoint” quadrature numerical routine, without re-scaling the
\int_{0}^{\infty}
integrals
and some of the last digits given in Table
2
might be slightly imprecise. The case
\nu=3
is precise and given by (
391
).
The table should be ideally re-calculated with a more sophisticated numerical quadrature, nevertheless, the Table can be used with confidence.

3
3

3

Notably, our
A_{3}(3)=1.4252
given by the semi-analytic (
391
),
slightly differs from the
A_{3}(3)=1.4272=0.7136\times 2
value given by
[
67
]
and cited also by
[
41
]

(their value should have been
0.7126
). Otherwise (perhaps surprisingly), our numerical results are consistent with
[
41
]
,
implying that the purely repulsive case is easy to integrate and that our precision can be improved easily.

Values
A_{l}(\nu)
for
repulsive
inter-particle force
1/r^{\nu}
, as a numerical solution of eq. (
386
). From the cases given,
note that the
A_{1}(\nu)
reaches a minimum around
\nu=9
and
the
A_{3}(\nu)
around
\nu=15
. However, frustratingly, the
A_{2}(\nu)
still did not reach minimum at
\nu=25
, so we were very pleased to discover that

[
